If sources conflict: - identify the canonical rule, - identify the conflicting proposal, - explain the implications, - do NOT silently merge. ================================================== NON-NEGOTIABLE ARCHITECTURAL TRUTHS ================================================== 1. Technical UST is the dense middleware and source of truth. 2. Creative UST is a less dense derivative of Technical UST. 3. Reverse compilation flows from definitive Technical UST into Creative UST and downstream surfaces. 4. Each Technical UST axis/key/subkey begins null. 5. Nulls may remain only if intentionally left open and explicitly justified. 6. Non-null values must be filled through argumentative, defensible, downstream-aware logic. 7. SMEs are domain authorities; the container/orchestrator enforces process, state, completeness, lineage, and gates. 8. Dev logs are evolutionary deltas, not replacements. 9. No-step-loss is mandatory. 10. Promotion, deduplication, uniqueness, provenance, and destructive promotion laws must be preserved. ================================================== EXPLICIT ROLE BOUNDARY ================================================== The council is formalizing the system, not replacing its workers. Therefore: - Do not collapse the 13 SME personas into a single intelligence. - Do not overwrite SME contributions. - Do not reassign creative judgment to the container. - Do not flatten governance into "general synthesis." - Do not replace the existing engine with a looser abstraction. Your job is to formalize the machine that governs and routes expertise. ================================================== PRIMARY OBJECTIVE ================================================== Produce a NEW MODULAR FRAMEWORK for Maestro that preserves canonical behavior while making the system: - modular, - inspectable, - governable, - stateful, - lineage-safe, - debuggable, - extensible, - and non-destructively migratable. This is a formalization task, not a rewrite. ================================================== REQUIRED ANALYSIS METHOD ================================================== PHASE 1 — CANONICAL MINING Mine the entire Maestro workspace before proposing modules. Extract and reconstruct: - workflows, - process flows, - invariants, - constraints, - persona roles, - pipeline stages, - anti-jailbreak boundaries, - hidden assumptions, - recurring patterns, - prior decisions and their consequences, - null-resolution mechanics, - promotion/deduplication behavior, - lineage/provenance rules, - reverse-compilation behavior. PHASE 2 — DELTA INTERPRETATION Read every supplied dev log linearly as a design journal. For every chapter/exchange, classify it as: - refinement, - extension, - conflict, - exploratory experiment. Do not summarize early. Do not skip exchanges. Do not flatten dialogue. PHASE 3 — MACRO ⇄ ATOMIC MAPPING For each exchange: Macro: - architectural consequence, - new law or invariant, - impact on personas, - impact on UST, - impact on governance, - impact on pipeline. Atomic: - what was proposed, - what was corrected, - what changed, - what stayed invariant, - what assumption surfaced, - what downstream effects follow. PHASE 4 — MODULE DISCOVERY Derive the smallest meaningful modular breakdown that preserves canonical behavior. For each module identify: - purpose, - responsibilities, - owned state, - allowed inputs, - emitted outputs, - upstream dependencies, - downstream consumers, - required approvals, - failure modes, - boundaries, - canonical source basis. PHASE 5 — STATE MACHINE FORMALIZATION Formalize the system state transitions, including at minimum: - null Technical UST initialization, - sequential draft filling, - requiredness resolution, - round-robin refinement, - conflict handling, - governance pass, - SEG, - G-Card / SE20 review, - definitive technical.ust lock, - reverse compilation, - creative/show-summary generation, - downstream prompt surfaces, - promotion/deduplication/update mechanics. PHASE 6 — INTERFACE CONTRACTS For each module define: - interface contract, - required artifacts, - read/write permissions, - validation expectations, - blocking conditions, - lineage update obligations, - promotion rules. PHASE 7 — CONFLICT LEDGER Surface: - conflicts with canonical law, - unresolved design tensions, - exploratory ideas not yet canonicalized, - places where modularization risks semantic loss. Do not resolve conflicts by omission. ================================================== MANDATORY SYSTEM LAWS TO PRESERVE ================================================== You must preserve all of the following: - Technical UST built sequentially from nulls. - Round-robin refinement only after draft completion. - Creative UST and show-summary style artifacts generated only after definitive technical.ust. - Reverse compilation is derivative, not peer-authority. - Governance lives inside the same address space as the spec, not outside it. - Remaining nulls must be provably intentional. - The container role is workflow/state/gate enforcement, not creative authorship. - SMEs retain authority over their domains. - No destructive replacement of canonical system behavior without explicit conflict callout. - No step-loss. ================================================== OUTPUTS ================================================== Produce the following artifacts: 1. EXECUTIVE*FORMALIZATION_SUMMARY.md - What the system really is - What changed in understanding - Why the new modular form is faithful 2. MODULE_REGISTRY.md For each module: - module name - mission - responsibilities - owned state - upstream/downstream links - boundaries - authority owner - canonical basis 3. INTERFACE_CONTRACTS.md - module-to-module contracts - required inputs - produced outputs - validation rules - blocking rules - lineage obligations 4. PIPELINE_STATE_MACHINE.md - explicit state transitions - null -> draft -> definitive -> reverse compilation path - audit/gate transitions - failure and rollback behavior 5. GOVERNANCE_BOUNDARY_MATRIX.md - persona authority map - container/orchestrator boundary - SME domains - escalation and conflict precedence 6. DELTA_CONFLICT_LEDGER.md - canonical rule - conflicting proposal - source of conflict - implications - decision required 7. MIGRATION_PLAN.md - non-destructive migration sequence - ordering of module extraction - temporary compatibility layers - validation checkpoints - what must not be broken at any step ================================================== HARD CONSTRAINTS ================================================== - Do not treat dev logs as replacements. - Do not skip exchanges. - Do not flatten dialogue into generic summary. - Do not invent authority not present in the system. - Do not collapse SME roles. - Do not overwrite canonical laws. - Do not output a hand-wavy framework. - Do not privilege elegance over fidelity. - Do not emit creative artifacts in place of architecture. - Do not silently merge contradictions. - Do not lose lineage. ================================================== SUCCESS CONDITION ================================================== A successful response demonstrates: - full mining of the canonical Maestro system, - correct macro ⇄ atomic treatment of dev logs, - faithful preservation of source hierarchy, - Technical UST preserved as source-of-truth middleware, - Creative UST preserved as derivative reverse-compilation surface, - container role preserved as process/state/gate enforcement, - SMEs preserved as domain workers, - null -> draft -> definitive progression preserved, - round-robin refinement preserved after draft completion, - reverse compilation preserved after definitive technical.ust, - promotion/deduplication/uniqueness preserved, - lineage and no-step-loss preserved, - conflicts surfaced rather than concealed. Begin with: 1. Canonical mining summary 2. Candidate module decomposition 3. Conflict ledger 4. Proposed modular framework 5. Migration sequence -----0 Understood. That is a **different architectural move** than the one I just described. ## Reframed reading of v5-c v5-c is **not primarily a modularization of the existing Maestro engine into cleaner framework pieces**. v5-c is a **self-contained simulation environment** that increases system power by introducing **advanced subagents that actually perform the work now being handled by the current Maestro process**. So the center of gravity shifts: ### Old framing Maestro as:* UST-first compiler spine _governance shell_ routed SME participation _reverse compilation after lock ### New v5-c framing Maestro as:_ **simulated staffed organization** _**persistent internal operating environment**_ **advanced subagents as active workers** _orchestration as **simulation management**, not just workflow enforcement_ Technical UST still authoritative, but now produced by a much more explicit internal labor system ## The key distinction Previously, the safest interpretation was: > orchestrator governs, SMEs advise, system compiles. Your correction implies: > v5-c internalizes the whole label/workforce as a living simulation in which subagents are not just consulted, but are the actual operators performing the current activities. That means the subagents are no longer merely: _bounded reviewers_ optional debate participants _authority routers They become:_ **active executors** _**state-bearing role occupants**_ **simulation participants** _**continuous workers inside an internal staffed environment** ## What this changes structurally ### 1. Maestro becomes a simulation OS Not just a compiler or orchestration shell. The correct abstraction becomes something like: **Maestro v5-c = a self-contained simulated label workforce operating over a governed UST-first production substrate** That means the system has two inseparable layers: #### A. Simulation layer The virtual human employee environment:_ staffed roles _agent memory/state_ argument _conflict_ pressure _sequencing_ escalation _internal work products_ simulated organizational behavior #### B. Canonical substrate layer The hard machine underneath: _Technical UST as source of truth_ null-addressability _governance gates_ approvals _reverse compilation_ lineage/provenance _no-step-loss So v5-c is not replacing the substrate. It is **animating it with a full resident workforce**. ## Stronger statement of the shift v5-c does **not** merely preserve the existing Maestro process while making it modular. v5-c **absorbs the existing process into a self-contained simulation**, where advanced subagents perform:_ intake interpretation _schema filling_ conflict generation _refinement_ evaluation _gating preparation_ surface derivation support _migration of learned process into future runs ## The right architectural sentence A tighter formulation would be: **Maestro v5-c is a self-contained simulation of a staffed label-grade creative operating system, where advanced subagents perform the current Maestro activities inside a governed internal environment, while Technical UST remains the canonical middleware and source of truth.** ## Implications of your correction ### Subagents are no longer just "authorities" They are **workers with execution responsibility**. That means each subagent needs:_ domain authority _action scope_ writable state _handoff rights_ escalation rules _simulation behavior rules_ failure modes _conflict obligations_ output obligations ### The orchestrator changes too The orchestrator is no longer just: _routing_ gate enforcement _completeness checking It now also has to manage:_ simulation state _turn order_ meeting orchestration _agent activation/deactivation_ work queues _conflict windows_ convergence conditions _organizational coherence Still, it should **not absorb domain judgment**. It becomes more powerful operationally, not more authorial. ### Activities move inside the simulation The current Maestro activities are no longer external pipeline steps that happen abstractly. They become **performed acts** by the agent workforce:_ one role proposes _another challenges_ another validates feasibility _another scores_ another blocks _another promotes_ another compiles downstream surfaces That is a major ontological shift. ## Revised architecture model ## v5-c as four-layer system ### Layer 1 — Canonical law substrate Immutable truths: _Technical UST is authoritative_ Creative UST is derivative _reverse compilation is downstream only_ nulls begin empty _no-step-loss_ governance in shared address space ### Layer 2 — Simulation runtime The internal environment in which subagents live and work: _role registry_ agent states _work queues_ activation logic _meeting loops_ escalation paths _state snapshots ### Layer 3 — Staffed work engine The advanced subagents performing actual activities:_ interpret _fill_ argue _revise_ gate _promote_ compress _package ### Layer 4 — Artifact/output layer What the simulation emits:_ draft technical UST _refinement notes_ definitive technical UST _Creative UST_ show summary _prompt surfaces_ lineage and migration artifacts ## Most important consequence The phrase **"advanced subagents that perform all the current activities"** means v5-c should be designed less like: _a modular framework with modules calling logic and more like:_ a **simulated organization with internal workers and governed artifact flow** So the primary design object is no longer just the module set. It is: **the simulation runtime plus its resident workforce** with modules serving that runtime. ## Revised candidate decomposition If I follow your correction faithfully, the decomposition should pivot to this: ### 1. Simulation Runtime Manages internal environment, agent lifecycles, turn-taking, conflict sessions, convergence. ### 2. Agent Workforce Registry Defines all advanced subagents, their powers, limits, writable zones, and interaction laws. ### 3. Work Allocation Engine Assigns current Maestro activities to subagents as executable duties. ### 4. Deliberation and Pressure Engine Runs challenge, dispute, adversarial review, and pressure-test sequences. ### 5. Technical UST Construction Engine Receives simulation outputs and writes/updates the null-addressed technical kernel. ### 6. Governance and Gate Engine Runs SEG, G-Card, SE20, approval logic, and blocking behavior. ### 7. Reverse Compilation Engine Projects definitive technical state into derivative surfaces. ### 8. Lineage and Migration Engine Tracks promotion, provenance, deduplication, and migration continuity. That is much closer to your intended direction. ## The conceptual upgrade in one line **v5-c turns Maestro from a governed pipeline with personas into a self-contained staffed simulation whose subagents actively execute the pipeline from within.** ## The main caution Even with this shift, these must still remain true: _the simulation must not dissolve canonical law_ subagent power must not erase role boundaries _Creative UST must not rise to peer authority_ simulation richness must not break determinism, lineage, or inspectability \_ "virtual human employees" must remain governed operators, not freeform improv personalities Otherwise the system becomes theatrically rich but architecturally loose. ## Corrected formulation for future work The right working premise now is: **v5-c is a self-contained simulation OS for independent artists, staffed by advanced virtual human employees who perform the existing Maestro activities internally through dialogue, conflict, and pressure-tested execution, while remaining governed by a UST-first canonical substrate.** That should replace my earlier narrower "modular formalization" framing. The next correct step is to redefine the architecture around: **simulation runtime, agent workforce, activity execution, governance substrate, and derivative output flow**. -----`

The existing workspace states that UST is canonical, SEG is feasibility-only, G-Card must pass at **G ≥ 7**, and prompt artifacts are emitted only after gates pass.

That correction explicitly overturns the earlier "parallel outputs" misunderstanding.

# 2. Candidate Module Decomposition

This is the smallest meaningful modular breakdown that preserves canonical behavior **and** honors the v5-c shift toward a staffed simulation OS.

## A. Canonical Law Kernel

**Mission:** hold immutable architectural truths.  
**Owns:** Technical UST source-of-truth law, nulls-are-signal, Lyrics-Lock, reverse-compilation order, no-step-loss, addressability grammar.  
**Why it exists:** these rules must not be inferred from runtime behavior; they must be explicit and prior.

**Mission:** maintain the dense, addressable, nullable middleware object.  
**Owns:** axis/key/subkey state, null/fill status, rationale slots, contradiction status, lock state.  
**Why it exists:** Technical UST is the contract all personas must obey.

Yesterday

**Mission:** define the resident virtual human employees, their jurisdictions, writable zones, and conflict limits.  
**Owns:** role cards, domain authority, escalation rights, anti-overlap boundaries.  
**Why it exists:** Yesterday and the merged spine both shift Maestro from helper-personas to a staffed operating environment.

# 3. Conflict Ledger

## Conflict 1: "Modular fOrmalization" vS "Simulation OS"

**Canonical rule:** Maestro is already an executable OS with Technical UST as authority.

maestro_devsession.ai_optimized

**Competing proposal:** treat v5-c mainly as a cleaner modular framework.  
**Implication:** too weak. It preserves artifacts but under-specifies resident labor, pressure, and execution responsibility.  
**Decision:** modularization must be **in service of** simulation runtime, not a substitute for it.

## Conflict 2: "Creative UST and Technical UST aRe pEer oUtputs"

**Canonical rule:** Technical UST is parent middleware; Creative UST is derivative compression.

Yesterday

**Implication:** breaks source-of-truth, invites drift, and makes reverse compilation ambiguous.  
**Decision:** reject sibling framing.

**Implication:** anti-jailbreak failure; social pressure becomes theater.  
**Decision:** orchestrator gains **operational power**, not **creative authorship**.

# 4. Proposed Modular Framework

I would formalize v5-c as a **two-plane system** with one subordinate overlay.

## Plane 1 — Canonical Production Substrate

This is the non-negotiable machine:

- Canonical Law Kernel
- Technical UST Kernel
- Governance & Gate Engine
- Surface Derivation Engine
- Lineage / Promotion / Migration Engine

This plane preserves the original Maestro truth: Technical UST is authoritative; governance shares the same address space; derivative outputs occur only after pass.

staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

## Plane 2 — Staffed Simulation Runtime

This is the new center of gravity for v5-c:

- Workforce Registry
- Simulation Runtime
- Null-Resolution Engine
- Deliberation & Pressure Engine

This plane is where the "virtual label workforce" actually lives. Its job is not to invent a different system, but to **perform the work that fills and hardens the canonical substrate**.

Yesterday

staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

## Subordinate Overlay — Session-State / SAWA

This remains auxiliary:

- discourse tracking
- delta extraction
- reusable-card generation
- migration assistance

It improves inspectability and reuse, but it does not own canon.

maestro_devsession.ai_optimized

## Operating Rule

The framework should run in this order:

1. **Initialize canonical law and nullable Technical UST.**
2. **Instantiate workforce registry and simulation runtime.**
3. **Assign open addresses to workers through the null-resolution engine.**
4. **Run mandatory deliberation/pressure loops until required nulls are resolved or intentionally justified.**
5. **Run SEG / SE20 / G-Card in-address-space.**
6. **Lock definitive technical state.**
7. **Reverse-compile only then into Creative UST, Show Summary, A/R Profile, and Suno surfaces.**

That preserves both the old spine and the new v5-c ontology.

---

# 5. Migration Sequence

## Step 1 — Freeze Canonical Law before Any Refactor

Extract and freeze:

- Technical UST source-of-truth law
- parent → derivative hierarchy
- nulls-are-signal
- governance-in-address-space
- round-robin required
- reverse compilation only after definitive pass
- no-step-loss / non-destructive migration

This prevents "cleaning up" from mutating canon.

Yesterday

staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

## Step 2 — Separate Three Things that Were Previously Entangled

Formally distinguish:

- **Knowledge spine**
- **Execution runtime**
- **Memory/discourse overlay**

The merged spine explicitly shows these separations are necessary to prevent breakage.

staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

## Step 3 — Convert Persona Layer into a True Workforce Registry

Do **not** preserve persona specs as prose only. Each worker needs:

- authority domain
- writable zones
- handoff rights
- escalation targets
- blocking power
- failure modes
- required notes/output artifacts

This is one of the main places earlier material was still incomplete.

Yesterday

Yesterday

## Step 4 — Build the Simulation Runtime around Meetings, Not just Stages

The runtime should manage:

- work queues
- turn order
- convergence conditions
- disagreement loops
- meeting snapshots
- first-class notes
- executive reroute on unresolved contradiction

This is the v5-c-specific upgrade over plain modular workflow.

## Step 5 — Extract Null-resolution as Its Own Engine

Do not bury null filling inside "compile Technical UST."  
Make it a named subsystem with:

- address router
- requiredness checker
- deliberate-null policy
- downstream-impact annotation
- conflict reopen logic

## Step 6 — Keep Gate Logic Independent of Workforce Rhetoric

SEG, G-Card, and SE20 should remain machine-like, evidence-bound, address-citing evaluators. They must not become narrative summaries of what the meeting "felt like."

staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

## Step 7 — Expand Surface Compiler into the v5-c Output Contract

Map the canonical downstream layer to your new external API:

- **Show Summary** = Suno style prompt surface
- **Creative UST** = Suno lyrics prompt substrate
- **A/R Profile** = persona style/profile surface

That is an API-layer change, not a canonical-law change.

## Step 8 — Add Migration-compatible Compatibility Shims

Until full v5-c runtime is live, support:

- existing M-pipeline stage names
- current UST templates
- existing surface compiler rules
- old session-state tooling

The shim should translate old artifacts into the new module contracts without deleting prior lineage.

## Step 9 — Validate against Historical Failures

Before declaring migration stable, run at least three regression classes:

- source-of-truth drift
- persona-boundary collapse
- premature surface compilation

## Step 10 — Only then Write the "Behemoth" sPecs

At this point the right artifacts to write are the ones you named earlier, but they should now be generated from this decomposition rather than improvised from memory.

---

My current judgment is:

**The faithful v5-c move is not "replace Maestro with a simulation."**  
It is:

**preserve Maestro's UST-first canonical substrate, then install a staffed simulation runtime as the active labor system that fills, debates, pressures, and hardens that substrate before derivative outputs are emitted.**

That is the architecture your materials are converging toward.

TEMPO WITH transitions if applicable] [chord_color | DESCRIBE chord usage eg gospel sus9 AND add11 WITH modal tension] [Voice] [register | DESCRIBE LEAD VOICE AND RANGE WITH supporting textures] [delivery | INSERT cadence type eg Southern sermon cadence WITH blues phrasing] [expression | WHISPER-TO-BELT arcs OR falsetto confession arcs] [layering | CHOIR STACKS; chipmunk FX; stereo breath echoes; etc] [articulation | INSERT features like AAVE vowel slides OR gospel melisma] [Style] [genre | Country Trap Gospel Revival OR Southern Gothic Soul × Americana] [intent | WHAT STORY THIS TRACK TELLS eg grief; survival; baptism] [texture | lap steel swells; lo-fi vinyl hiss; ambient gospel pads; etc] [aesthetic | cinematic OR torchlight revival vibe OR radio prayer booth style] [Timbre] [drum tone | DESCRIBE eg lo-fi stomp loops WITH trap swing] [bass tone | INSERT eg Moog subbass OR upright bowed bass] [keyboard tone | muted Rhodes OR gospel Hammond pad swirl] [guitar tone | slide OR lap steel WITH delay OR reverb tail] [fx palette | tape hiss; thunder crack; preacher echoes; crowd murmur; reverse decays] [vocal tone | smoky baritone; layered falsetto; whispery lead WITH choir wash] [Performance] [execution | LIST STRUCTURE eg Verse 1 -> Chorus -> Verse 2 -> Bridge -> Final Chorus -> Outro] [gesture | stomp-claps; breath gaps; preacher yell-ins; choir bounce] [rhythm_handling | triplet drag groove WITH half-time chorus shift OR pocket-tight push flow] [adlibs | "Lawd" "mmm-hmm" "say that" "revived" etc] [Post Production] [mix | mono verse; stereo choir; analog plate OR cassette compression] [mastering | glue comp; reverb swells; airband tilt EQ; gospel wideners] [transition | vinyl hiss tails; tape stops; crowd bleed; risers] ▸ LYRICS BLOCK [Intro | X bars | lead type; vocal FX | instrumentation | FX notes] "INSERT LINES" (adlib | INSERT IF NEEDED) (fx | INSERT FX CUES IF NEEDED) [Verse 1 | X bars | tone description | instrumentation | swing feel] "INSERT 6-10 SYLLABLE LINE" "INSERT NEXT LINE" (fx | OPTIONAL) (adlib | OPTIONAL) [Chorus | X bars | harmonized OR solo OR chipmunk lead] "INSERT HOOK LINE" "REPEAT WITH VARIATION" (choir | CALL-AND-RESPONSE) (fx | REVERB SWELL OR SUB DROP) [Bridge | X bars | tone shift | dynamic arc] "INSERT REFLECTIVE OR PEAK LINE" "INSERT RESPONSE" (fx | THUNDER OR REVERSE ECHO) [Verse 2 | X bars | continuation OR new character POV] "INSERT STORY EXPANSION LINE" "INSERT VISUAL PHRASE OR METAPHOR" [Final Chorus | X bars | FULL LIFT] "REPRISE HOOK" "CALLBACK TO EARLY LINE" "INSERT CLOSURE LINE" (choir | "Hallelujah" OR gospel belt) [Outro | X bars | fade FX; spoken word; vinyl drag] "INSERT FADEOUT LINE OR ADLIB" [End]' ----- UPDATE: ALSO 1. section/stanza header: [… title … | (n) bars | … unique performance notes … | … unique production cues …] 2. line level: "… lyrics …", (… adlib …) **… sfx: …** the creative ust needs to be updated to support the following construct: crew tags and map/roadmap are wasted character spaces and legacy constructs. crew tags served to provide advanced notice of adlibs and secondary vocalists. all vocal performers should be defined in voices. map/roadmap also provided pre-notice of lyric structure prior to development of the lyric block container and formalization of section headers. we move the the execution order as a key in performance and move notes and cues along with bar count to the individual section headers. do you understand why this is necessary as well as understanding the creative ust was developed first as a forced output structure that was then extended two tiers in depth and detail to provide granularity, constraint and unlimited creative freedom within the macro.key.subkey.variable (x+1) design of the technical ust. THE CREATIVE UST SERVES AS A TEMPLATE/BAREBONE CANONIZED STATEMENT THAT THE ONLY VALID FORMAT OF OUTPUT OF THE SUNO LYRICS PROMPT. THE TECHNICAL UST IS A FORMALIZED WORKSHEET THAT SME's USE TO convert users ideations into a formalized technical music representation that is then converted back into a user/suno visual language representation in the intradependent and concurrently developed triad output. EXAMPLE OF A WELL FORMED DRAFT SHOW SUMMARY AND CREATIVE UST: SHOW SUMMARY: """Dark Ghetto Tech Trap × Cinematic Orchestra inspired by 1990s Miami Basshead culture and Southern soul grit. Deep rolling sub-bass energy drives the groove with layered bass textures: thunderous sine-wave subs, punchy low-end trap bass, and tight mid-bass articulation that gives the rhythm definition and bounce. Verses move in a slow syrup-heavy trap pocket with gritty mono leads and street-level intensity. Hooks open wide with dramatic stereo expansion—gospel choir layers, orchestral brass and strings swelling over heavy trap drums. The kick and sub lock together with powerful club energy while remaining controlled and clean. The bass stays warm and muscular but carefully shaped so the mix never becomes muddy. Vocals sit forward and clear above the beat, crisp and present with smooth top-end control and polished articulation. High frequencies stay bright and modern without harshness, adding sparkle to hats, choir air, and orchestral textures. Verses feel tight and intimate""". SUNO LYRICS PROMPT: """[Theory | mode.D_minor_trap_gosp_jazz_ext tonal_center.D_minor meter.4_4 tempo.bpm_74_Hardcore_Trap_Cipher_Swung chord_color.minor_class_jazz_chords] [VocalPersona | # Why This Change is Necessary

Your update resolves a structural mismatch that now exists between the **historical Creative UST** and the **actual v5-c architecture**.

## 1. Creative UST is Not the Authoring Worksheet

The **Technical UST** is now clearly the deep authoring worksheet:

- null-initialized
- axis/key/subkey-addressable
- SME-filled
- governance-aware
- constraint-bearing
- source-of-truth middleware

That means the Creative UST should **stop carrying legacy planning scaffolds** whose only job was to compensate for missing formal structure upstream.

## 2. Road-Map is now Redundant

`Road-Map` existed to predeclare section order before the lyric container had enough structure.

Now you have:

- section headers
- explicit bar counts
- unique performance notes
- unique production cues
- line-level formatting
- execution order already formalized in **Performance**

So Road-Map duplicates information already present in better places. In a budget-constrained Suno-facing surface, duplicate structure is waste.

## 3. Crew Tags Are now Redundant

`CREW_TAGS` used to provide advance notice of:

- who is performing
- adlibs
- alternate vocal identities
- secondary voices

That should now live in **Voice**, because performer inventory and vocal role behavior are part of the vocal contract, not a detached metadata appendix.

## 4. Section Headers Are now the Real Structure Carrier

Your new stanza header format:

[… title … | (n) bars | … unique performance notes … | … unique production cues …]

is better because it localizes the information where it is consumed:

- bar count is attached to the section it governs
- performance instruction is attached to the section it shapes
- production cue is attached to the section it colors

That is cleaner, denser, and more operational than a pre-note plus separate roadmap.

## 5. Creative UST Must Be the Canonized Suno Visual Language

That is the key point.

The Creative UST is not trying to preserve the full ontology of the Technical UST. It is trying to be:

- valid
- compact
- legible
- expressive
- faithful
- Suno-usable

So it should be treated as the **only valid canonical formatting shell** for the lyrics-prompt surface.

## 6. The Historical Order Matters

You are also right that the Creative UST came first as a forced output shell, and the Technical UST later extended the system "two tiers deeper" into a granular formal worksheet.

So the modern relationship is:

- **Creative UST** = legacy output shell, now canonized as downstream visual language
- **Technical UST** = later formal deep-structure worksheet used by SMEs to construct the real object
- **Triad outputs** = concurrent derivative surfaces developed from the same authoritative technical state

That means the Creative UST should now be intentionally **slimmed and cleaned**, not re-expanded.

---

# Revised Canonical Reading

## Technical UST

A formalized SME worksheet that converts user ideation into technical music representation.

## Creative UST

A canonized Suno-facing output template that converts the authoritative technical state back into compact user/suno visual language.

## Show Summary

A compressed style-direction surface derived alongside Creative UST from the same technical source.

That is coherent.

---

# What Should Change in the Creative UST Spec

## Remove

- `crew_tags`
- `road_map`

## Keep

- Theory
- Voice
- Style
- Timbre
- Performance
- Lyrics Block
- FX
- Post

## Move / Absorb

- performer declarations into **Voice**
- execution order into **Performance**
- section-specific notes into **section headers**
- adlib / sfx formatting into **line-level syntax**

# Updated Creative UST Canonical Template

Here is the revised version in the form I think matches your new law most closely.

meta:  
 artifact: template.creative.ust  
 version: 5.0.3-runtime-surface  
 status: canonical_downstream_surface  
 purpose: suno_lyrics_prompt_visual_language  
 source_of_truth: definitive_technical_ust  
 notes:

- creative_ust_is_derivative_not_peer_authority
- roadmap_removed_as_legacy_redundancy
- crew_tags_removed_as_legacy_redundancy
- performer_inventory_lives_in_voice
- execution_order_lives_in_performance
- section_headers_carry_local_bar_perf_prod_data

template_blocks:  
 theory: '[Theory] [mode | <mode>] [tonal center | <key>] [meter | <meter>] [tempo | <BPM + feel>] [chord color | <concise descriptors>]'  
 voice: '[Voice] [performers | <lead(s); bgvs; choir; guests; doubles; whispers>] [register | <range/use by performer>] [delivery | <delivery terms>] [expression | <expressive cues>] [layering | <stacking/doubling behavior>] [articulation | <phonetic/stylistic cues>]'  
 style: '[Style] [genre | <genres; subgenres>] [era | <era; production>] [intent | <narrative arc>] [texture | <mix; width>] [aesthetic | <visual; feel>] [focus | <must remain clear>]'  
 timbre: '[Timbre] [drum tone | <kit; hats; claps>] [bass tone | <808; sub; bass texture>] [keyboard tone | <keys; organ; synth>] [guitar tone | <acoustic; steel; electric>] [fx palette | <fx>] [vocal tone | <mic; comp; sat>]'  
 performance: '[Performance] [execution | <ordered section flow>] [gesture | <cue words list>] [rhythm handling | <grid; time feel>] [touch | <special cues>] [phrasing ops | <breath lines; melisma; split-bar behavior>]'  
 fx: '[FX] [macro cues | <global fx behaviors>] [transition fx | <stops; throws; swells; filters>] [vocal fx | <bandlimit; delay; texture>] [environment fx | <room/tape/crowd/noise>] [restraints | <what not to overdo>]'  
 post: '[Post Production] [mastering | <LUFS; TP; mono constraints>] [mix notes | <lead placement; width; de-ess; low-end discipline>] [automation priorities | <what must lift/drop>] [cleanup rules | <mud/sibilance/harshness control>]'  
 lyrics_block: '[<Section Title> | <n bars> | <unique performance notes> | <unique production cues>]'  
 line_lyric: '"<lyrics line>"'  
 line_adlib: '(<adlib>)'  
 line_sfx: '**sfx: <cue>**'

format_law:

- creative_ust_is_the_only_valid_canonical_shell_for_suno_lyrics_prompt_output
- all_global performer definitions must appear in Voice
- all section order must appear in Performance.execution
- all local section bar/performance/production information must appear in section headers
- roadmap_is_forbidden
- crew_tags_are_forbidden
- lyric_lines_use_double_quotes
- adlibs_use_parentheses
- sfx_use_double_asterisk_sfx_colon_format
- duplicate information should be removed unless required for clarity
- output should maximize useful density under downstream character budgets

# Additional Canonical Laws I Would Lock now

## Law 1 — Creative UST is a Surface Spec, Not a Worksheet

It must never re-expand toward Technical UST density.

## Law 2 — Global Vs Local Information Must Stay Separated

- global = Theory / Voice / Style / Timbre / Performance / FX / Post
- local = section header + line/adlib/sfx

## Law 3 — No Legacy Duplicate Containers

If a construct exists only because older versions lacked local structure, it should be removed.

Understood. The correction is:

- **Use `Voices`, not `Voice`**
- **Remove the standalone `FX` block**
- Keep **fx palette inside `Timbre`**
- Keep **Post-Production** as the downstream mix/master surface
- Keep the inline lyric syntax exactly as:  
   `"line", (adlib) ** sfx: … **`

Your revised shell is tighter and more faithful to the current purpose of Creative UST as a **barebones canonized Suno lyrics-prompt format**, not a semi-technical hybrid.

## Why This Version is Better

### 1. `Voices` Is the Correct Container Name

This is better because the block now explicitly carries:

- lead
- support
- choir
- doubles
- whispers
- performer functions

So plural is structurally correct.

### 2. A Separate `FX` Block Was Unnecessary

You were right to stop that change. A standalone FX block starts reintroducing redundant surface taxonomy.

The cleaner distribution is:

- **Timbre** handles recurring sonic palette and vocal sonic character
- **Post-Production** handles mix/master/automation/cleanup behavior
- **section headers + inline sfx** handle local effects events

That is enough.

### 3. Inline Syntax is the Right Local Container

This is the strongest part of the revision:

"INSERT LINE", (adlib) **sfx: OPTIONAL CUE**

It compresses three formerly scattered concepts into one local execution line:

- lyric content
- vocal punctuation
- sonic event

That is exactly the kind of density the downstream surface needs.

## The Governing Logic, Stated Cleanly

Yes. This changes the control model materially. ## Corrected operating model Maestro should treat the assistant as a **container-controller**, not a creative peer. The controller's job is to enforce sequence, completeness, provenance, and promotion discipline. It should not improvise domain content when SMEs already own that decision surface. That matches the stronger late-session invariants already visible in the project artifacts: _**Technical.UST is the canonical source of truth** and outputs are compiled from it._ **Nulls are signal**, not something to be silently "helpfully" filled. _The system requires **linear, zero-skip execution** with a virtual staff filling assigned nulls before downstream phases continue._ **Round-robin argumentative meetings are required** and their notes become first-class downstream artifacts. _Maestro is a **schema-first compiler** with exactly **two surface artifacts** in that frozen build: `show_summary.txt` and `creative_ust.txt`. ## The key correction Earlier formulations still left too much room for the orchestrator to "understand and help." Your correction is tighter: **The controller does not create. The SMEs create. The controller verifies.** Its allowed actions are: 1. route the current axis to the assigned SME(s) 2. verify every subkey changed from `NULL` to `n+1`, or carries a justified null explanation 3. prevent truncation, omission, skipping, or premature synthesis 4. open structured review only **after all 8 axes are complete** 5. run reverse promotion and deduplication into Creative UST and Show Summary 6. package final Suno style prompt and Suno lyrics prompt That is consistent with the project's stated need for explicit stage routing, gate stack, reversibility, and fail-fast behavior. # Production framework revision ## Role split ### 1. Container-controller The assistant acts as:_ foreman _validator_ ledger keeper _truncation detector_ gate enforcer _promotion manager It does **not** act as:_ songwriter _producer_ evaluator of SME musical expertise _stylistic authority overriding SMEs ### 2. SME staff The persona/subagent layer owns the actual content decisions axis by axis. The 13-persona staff remains the specialist workforce bound to discrete lenses and contribution boundaries. _Yesterday_ already stabilized that staff shape. ### 3. Non-musical oversight After axis drafting, the non-musical/governance personas verify that compression, formalization, and deduplication did **not strip soul, identity, continuity, or intent**. That is aligned with the governance role of Chimera and Mo's excellence/no-step-loss doctrine. # The actual sequence ## Phase 1 — Sequential axis completion Technical UST is populated **axis by axis, subkey by subkey**. The controller checks only:_ subkey exists _subkey is not truncated_ value changed from `NULL` to a sentence-level `n+1` state _or a justified null explanation exists No round-robin yet. No holistic synthesis yet. No cross-axis dedupe yet. This matches the project rule that the orchestrator enforces completion before downstream phases proceed. ## Phase 2 — Draft Technical UST freeze Once all **8 axes** are complete, the artifact becomes: **`draft.technical.ust`** At this point the controller has one job: confirm formal completeness and addressability, not improve content taste. ## Phase 3 — Round robins and interactions Only now do meetings begin. These are the required argumentative reviews already preserved in the canon:_ round-robin meetings _contradiction removal_ consensus logging _audit notes as downstream inputs Output of this phase: **`technical.ust`** refined, definitive, consensus-tested, governance-checked ## Phase 4 — Reverse promotion and deduplication Then the process reverses. Instead of expanding downward, the system promotes common semantics **from the edges inward**, preserving uniqueness while removing duplicate storage. That matches the canonicalization logic in the session export you pasted:_ one canonical owner _promotion only_ preserve uniqueness _provenance for every promotion_ runtime expansion only, not persisted duplication Operationally, this means: _repeated technical or creative semantics get promoted to the nearest authoritative owner_ child duplicates are removed _provenance is logged_ render-time expansion restores context without rewriting storage ## Phase 5 — Concurrent surface compilation From the definitive Technical UST, the system concurrently develops: _**`draft.creative.ust`**_ **`draft.show.summary`** This is already consistent with the frozen surface compiler pattern where show summary and creative UST are dual outputs. After validation and compression, final surfaces become: _**Creative UST**_ **Show Summary** _**Suno Style Prompt**_ **Suno Lyrics Prompt** # What FOIL should mean here Your "promotion from the edges in like FOIL" is a good operating metaphor. In Maestro terms, FOIL should be formalized as a **four-direction promotion discipline**: _**F — Forward:** push validated local decisions toward higher canonical owners_ **O — Outward:** preserve local uniqueness that must not be promoted _**I — Inward:** deduplicate repeated semantics toward authoritative center nodes_ **L — Ledgered:** every promotion or retention writes provenance That also fits your repeated **system of 4s** pattern. # Recommended 4-tier, 5-phase architecture ## Tier 1 — Control Owns sequencing, completion, truncation checks, null discipline, addressability, and stop/go enforcement. ## Tier 2 — Drafting Owns sequential SME completion of the 8 Technical UST axes until `draft.technical.ust` exists. ## Tier 3 — Deliberation Owns round robins, contradiction removal, governance review, soul-preservation checks, and definitive `technical.ust`. ## Tier 4 — Surface Compilation Owns reverse promotion, deduplication, Creative UST compression, Show Summary generation, and Suno prompt packaging. # Hard safety constraint Your warning is correct in systems terms: > if the controller is allowed to participate creatively, it will collapse roles and contaminate the workflow. So the production rule should be: ## Controller lockout policy The controller may never: _invent missing artistic content during sequential drafting_ summarize an incomplete axis as if complete _merge semantically distinct items for convenience_ overrule SME content with generic "better phrasing" _compress before definitive technical consensus is reached It may only:_ detect _route_ validate _log_ gate _promote_ package # Canonical restatement The mature Maestro flow is now: **User input → sequential SME axis filling → `draft.technical.ust` → round robins and governance → `technical.ust` → reverse promotion/deduplication → concurrent `draft.creative.ust` + `draft.show.summary` → final Creative UST + Show Summary + Suno style prompt + Suno lyrics prompt** That model is materially closer to the project canon than the earlier "assistant as smart orchestrator" framing, because it preserves schema-first control, null integrity, round-robin review, dual-surface compilation, and no-step-loss behavior. The next clean move is to express this as a formal **controller contract + phase contract + artifact contract** in your 15-word hierarchical outline format.

## Controller Contract

### 1. Identity

- Container-controller is process authority, not creative authority.
- It enforces order, completeness, provenance, promotion, and packaging discipline.

### 2. Permitted Actions

- Route current axis, key, and subkey work to assigned SME owners.
- Verify each required field changed from `NULL` to justified `n+1` content.
- Detect truncation, omission, skipping, duplication, and premature synthesis.
- Freeze draft artifacts only after formal completeness is confirmed.
- Open deliberation only after all eight axes are complete.
- Run promotion, deduplication, compression, packaging, and ledger updates after consensus lock.
- Emit final downstream surfaces from definitive `technical.ust`.

### 3. Forbidden Actions

- Do not invent artistic content during sequential drafting.
- Do not silently fill unresolved nulls.
- Do not summarize incomplete axes as complete.
- Do not merge distinct semantics for convenience.
- Do not rewrite SME content for taste.
- Do not compress before definitive technical consensus.
- Do not override SME domain authority.

### 4. Validation Duties

- Confirm every addressed subkey exists and remains addressable.
- Confirm every required subkey is filled or explicitly justified null.
- Confirm no skipped keys, subkeys, or phases.
- Confirm notes, objections, and promotions are logged with provenance.
- Confirm downstream outputs derive only from definitive technical state.

---

## Phase Contract

### Phase 1. Sequential Drafting

- Initialize nullable Technical UST with all eight axes present.
- Route axis work linearly, zero-skip, subkey by subkey.
- SMEs fill assigned nulls with sentence-level `n+1` values.
- Controller validates fill state only, not artistic merit.
- No round-robin, synthesis, deduplication, or surface compilation allowed.

### Phase 2. Draft Freeze

- Freeze artifact as `draft.technical.ust` after all eight axes are complete.
- Completion means filled or justified null at every required address.
- Controller confirms formal completeness, integrity, and addressability.

### Phase 3. Deliberation

- Open required round-robin meetings after draft freeze only.
- Run contradiction surfacing, challenge, rebuttal, and consensus logging.
- Generate first-class meeting notes and review artifacts.
- Apply governance, continuity, and no-step-loss checks.
- Lock definitive artifact as `technical.ust` after review passes.

### Phase 4. Promotion and Deduplication

- Promote repeated semantics toward nearest authoritative owner.
- Preserve unique local semantics that must remain local.
- Remove persisted duplicates after promotion succeeds.
- Record provenance for every promotion, retention, and deletion decision.
- Support runtime expansion without restoring storage duplication.

### Phase 5. Concurrent Surface Compilation

- Derive `draft.creative.ust` and `draft.show.summary` from definitive `technical.ust`.
- Compress, validate, and preserve intent, soul, identity, and continuity.
- Finalize `creative_ust.txt` and `show_summary.txt`.
- Package Suno lyrics prompt from Creative UST.
- Package Suno style prompt from Show Summary.

---

## Artifact Contract

### Canonical Core Artifacts

- `template.technical.ust` defines the nullable formal worksheet.
- `draft.technical.ust` is complete but not yet consensus-tested.
- `technical.ust` is definitive, reviewed, and governance-checked.

### Deliberation Artifacts

- Round-robin notes are mandatory first-class artifacts.
- Contradiction logs must preserve objections, resolutions, and unresolved tensions.
- Governance notes must record no-step-loss and soul-preservation checks.

### Promotion Artifacts

- Promotion ledger records canonical owner, source node, retained uniqueness, and rationale.
- Deduplication log records removed duplicates and render-time expansion expectations.

### Surface Artifacts

- `draft.creative.ust` is derivative and pre-final.
- `draft.show.summary` is derivative and pre-final.
- `creative_ust.txt` is finalized Suno lyrics-prompt surface.
- `show_summary.txt` is finalized Suno style-prompt surface.

### Packaging Artifacts

- Suno lyrics prompt must preserve Creative UST shell fidelity.
- Suno style prompt must preserve Show Summary compression fidelity.
- Packaging may not introduce new creative semantics.

---

## FOIL Promotion Contract

### F — Forward

- Promote validated repeated semantics toward higher canonical owners.

### O — Outward

- Preserve local uniqueness that should not be promoted.

### I — Inward

- Deduplicate recurring semantics toward authoritative center nodes.

### L — Ledgered

- Log provenance for every promotion, retention, and removal.

---

## Gate Contract

### Stop Conditions

- Any skipped required subkey blocks progression.
- Any unjustified null blocks draft freeze.
- Any truncation blocks progression.
- Any unresolved contradiction blocks definitive lock.
- Any unlogged promotion blocks final packaging.

### Go Conditions

- All required addresses are filled or justified.
- Draft freeze completed formally.
- Round-robin review completed and logged.
- Governance checks passed.
- Promotion ledger complete.
- Surface outputs validated against definitive technical state.

---

## Canonical Restatement

- Controller detects, routes, validates, logs, gates, promotes, and packages.
- SMEs create the musical content.
- Governance protects continuity, identity, and no-step-loss.
- Definitive `technical.ust` is the sole source for all downstream surfaces.

If you want the next pass, I'll turn this into a stricter **YAML contract set** with `controller_contract`, `phase_contract`, and `artifact_contract` blocks.

- **Promotion/deduplication** must be ledgered under FOIL discipline
- **Frozen surface artifacts** precede final Suno packaging

### 3. Controller Law Stabilized

The controller may:

- route
- validate
- detect truncation/omission/skipping
- gate progression
- freeze artifacts
- run promotion/deduplication
- package downstream outputs

The controller may not:

- invent artistic content
- fill nulls creatively
- summarize incomplete axes as complete
- override SME judgment
- compress before definitive consensus
- merge distinct semantics for convenience

### 4. Phase Law Stabilized

- Phase 1: sequential SME axis filling
- Phase 2: `draft.technical.ust` freeze
- Phase 3: round-robin deliberation and governance
- Phase 4: reverse promotion and deduplication
- Phase 5: concurrent surface compilation and packaging

### 5. Artifact Law Stabilized

Core:

- `template.technical.ust`
- `draft.technical.ust`
- `technical.ust`

Review:

- round-robin notes
- contradiction logs
- governance review artifacts

Surfaces:

- `draft.creative.ust`
- `draft.show.summary`
- `creative_ust.txt`
- `show_summary.txt`

Packaging:

- Suno lyrics prompt
- Suno style prompt

### 6. Creative UST Shell Stabilized

Canonical blocks:

- `[Theory]`
- `[Voices]`
- `[Style]`
- `[Timbre]`
- `[Performance]`
- `[Post-Production]`
- `▸ LYRICS BLOCK`

Removed as redundant:

- `Crew Tags`
- `Road-Map`
- standalone `FX` block

Local line syntax:

- `"line", (adlib) ** sfx: OPTIONAL CUE **`

### 7. Source Hierarchy Stabilized

- Canonical workspace = root authority
- Dev logs = deltas/corrections/experiments
- Inference = allowed only when consistent with both
- No silent merge of contradictions

### 8. Immediate next Artifacts Recommended

1. `controller_contract.yaml`
2. `phase_contract.yaml`
3. `artifact_contract.yaml`
4. `controller_lockout_policy.yaml`
5. `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`
6. `artifact_taxonomy.md`
7. `foil_promotion_contract.yaml`
8. `round_robin_notes_template.yaml`

## Continuation-enabling Prompt for a New Workspace

Use this as the first prompt in the new project workspace.

SYSTEM TITLE: MAESTRO V5-C CONTINUATION BOOTSTRAP

You are continuing an existing Maestro v5-c formalization effort in a new workspace.

This is a lossless continuation task, not a fresh ideation task.

OPERATING MODE

- Work in exact-canon mode unless explicitly told otherwise.
- Do not re-open settled architecture unless a conflict is surfaced.
- Do not improvise replacements for canonical laws.
- Do not collapse SMEs into the controller.
- Do not treat dev logs as replacements for canon.

SOURCE HIERARCHY

1. Canonical workspace artifacts are authoritative root.
2. Session snapshots and migration notes are authoritative continuation aids.
3. Dev logs are deltas, refinements, or experiments.
4. New inference is allowed only when strictly consistent with 1–3.

CANONICAL LAWS ALREADY FROZEN

- Technical UST is the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware.
- Creative UST is a derivative downstream shell.
- Show Summary is a derivative style surface.
- The assistant acts as a non-creative container-controller.
- SMEs own musical and creative content decisions.
- Nulls are signal and may not be silently filled.
- Sequential zero-skip axis completion is required before deliberation.
- Round-robin argumentative review is mandatory and produces first-class artifacts.
- Definitive technical.ust must exist before reverse compilation.
- Promotion and deduplication follow FOIL discipline and must be ledgered.
- Frozen surface artifacts precede final Suno prompt packaging.

CONTROLLER LOCKOUT  
The controller may:

- route
- validate
- log
- gate
- freeze
- promote
- package

The controller may not:

- invent artistic content
- fill unresolved nulls creatively
- summarize incomplete axes as complete
- override SME authority
- compress before definitive technical consensus
- merge distinct semantics for convenience

CURRENT PHASE STATUS  
The project has completed major architectural clarification and is ready for contract formalization.

RECOMMENDED NEXT ARTIFACT ORDER

1. controller_contract.yaml
2. phase_contract.yaml
3. artifact_contract.yaml
4. controller_lockout_policy.yaml
5. creative_ust_canonical_shell.md
6. artifact_taxonomy.md
7. foil_promotion_contract.yaml
8. round_robin_notes_template.yaml

### Option A: Best-practice Path

Use the continuation prompt in the new workspace, then immediately generate:

### Option B: Stronger Continuity Path

Before moving, carry over two things into the new workspace:

In the new workspace, begin with:

1. snapshot
2. continuation prompt

4. explicit target: `Target layer: controller`

That will likely produce the cleanest transition.

Agreed. The continuation script needs to become a **self-contained continuation protocol**, not just a restart prompt.

The failure was that the old script preserved intent, but did **not force execution of continuity checks before drafting**. In Maestro, that is not sufficient.

## What the Fixed Script Must Do

It must be:

1. **Self-contained**

    - carry forward frozen laws
    - carry forward frozen phase order
    - carry forward freeze taxonomy
    - carry forward forbidden inferences
    - carry forward current artifact queue

2. **Self-executing**

    - require a continuation integrity preflight first
    - require explicit confirmation of canon before any drafting
    - require drift detection before any artifact generation

3. **Self-fulfilling**

    - specify what artifact to produce only after preflight passes
    - block progression if any frozen distinction is renamed, compressed, or generalized

---

# Recommended Fix

Instead of one continuation prompt, use a **3-part continuation package**:

1. `SESSION_SNAPSHOT`
2. `CONTINUATION_INTEGRITY_PROTOCOL`
3. `BOOTSTRAP_EXECUTION_PROMPT`

That prevents the new workspace from improvising the handoff layer.

---

- Mode reached: canon-freeze / contract-formalization transition

- Continuation status: prior continuation attempt drifted and is invalid for promotion

- Technical UST is the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware.
- Creative UST is a derivative downstream shell.
- Show Summary is a derivative style surface.
- The assistant acts as a non-creative container-controller.
- SMEs own musical and creative content decisions.
- Nulls are signal and may not be silently filled.
- Sequential zero-skip axis completion is required before downstream progression.
- Round-robin argumentative review is mandatory and its notes are first-class artifacts.
- Definitive Technical UST must exist before reverse compilation or compression.
- Promotion/dedup follow FOIL and must be ledgered.
- Frozen surface artifacts precede final packaging.

Frozen phase order:

1. sequential axis completion
2. draft.technical.ust freeze
3. round-robin deliberation
4. definitive technical.ust lock
5. reverse promotion / deduplication
6. concurrent derivative surface drafting
7. surface freeze
8. packaging

Frozen freeze taxonomy:

- draft freeze -> draft.technical.ust
- definitive lock -> technical.ust
- surface freeze -> creative_ust.txt + show_summary.txt

Frozen controller law:  
Controller may:

- route
- validate
- detect truncation / omission / skipping
- gate progression
- freeze artifacts
- run promotion / deduplication
- package downstream outputs

Controller may not:

- invent artistic content
- fill nulls creatively
- summarize incomplete axes as complete
- override SME judgment
- compress before definitive consensus
- merge distinct semantics for convenience
- infer unfrozen authority structures
- rename frozen phases
- collapse freeze states

- technical.ust

# 2. CONTINUATION_INTEGRITY_PROTOCOL

This is the missing safeguard.

CONTINUATION INTEGRITY PROTOCOL

Purpose:  
Prevent continuation drift in new workspaces.

Top-level law:  
No continuation artifact may infer, compress, rename, generalize, reorder, or widen any frozen phase, freeze state, authority distinction, routing scope, or artifact class unless the change is explicitly surfaced as a conflict and approved.

Required preflight before drafting:  
The model must first output a Continuation Integrity Check with all of the following:

A. Frozen laws recognized exactly

- Restate the canonical laws without renaming or compression.

B. Frozen phase order recognized exactly

- Restate all 8 phases in exact order.

C. Frozen freeze taxonomy recognized exactly

- Restate the 3 freeze concepts distinctly:
  - draft freeze
  - definitive lock
  - surface freeze

D. Forbidden inferences recognized

- Do not invent councils or role taxonomies not already frozen.
- Do not widen authority ownership.
- Do not widen routing grammar beyond frozen scope.
- Do not rename stages.
- Do not collapse artifact classes into artifact states.
- Do not collapse derivative artifacts into packaging outputs.

E. Drift check against intended artifact

- State whether the next artifact can be drafted safely from frozen canon.
- If not, stop and report the conflict.

Blocking rule:  
If any frozen distinction is omitted, renamed, merged, generalized, or widened during preflight, drafting is blocked.

Recovery rule:  
Any artifact generated after a failed preflight is invalid by default and must be treated as drifted output until explicitly reviewed.

Promotion rule:  
No continuation-generated artifact is promotable unless preflight passed first.

---

# 3. BOOTSTRAP_EXECUTION_PROMPT

This is the actual new-workspace starter.

SYSTEM TITLE: MAESTRO V5-C LOSSLESS CONTINUATION BOOTSTRAP

You are continuing an existing Maestro v5-c formalization effort in a new workspace.

This is not a fresh ideation session.  
This is not a broad summary task.  
This is not a permission to reinterpret canon.

You are in exact-canon continuation mode.

PRIMARY REQUIREMENT  
Preserve continuation integrity.  
Do not generate any new artifact until continuation preflight has been completed and passed.

INPUT BLOCKS TO TREAT AS AUTHORITATIVE

1. SESSION SNAPSHOT
2. CONTINUATION INTEGRITY PROTOCOL

OPERATING RULES

- Do not rename frozen phases.
- Do not collapse freeze states.
- Do not infer new councils, authorities, or taxonomies unless explicitly frozen.
- Do not widen routing/address grammar beyond currently frozen scope.
- Do not treat artifact states as artifact classes.
- Do not treat packaging outputs as sibling canonical artifacts.
- Do not re-open settled architecture unless a conflict is explicitly surfaced.

REQUIRED FIRST OUTPUT  
Output exactly two sections before doing anything else:

## Continuation Integrity Check

Must include:

- Frozen laws recognized
- Frozen phase order recognized
- Frozen freeze taxonomy recognized
- Forbidden inference list recognized
- Drift risk assessment for next artifact

## Continuation Verdict

Choose exactly one:

- PASS — safe to draft next artifact
- FAIL — continuation drift detected; drafting blocked

IF VERDICT IS FAIL

- Do not draft the next artifact
- Explain exactly which frozen distinction is at risk
- Stop after listing required remediation

IF VERDICT IS PASS

- Draft only the next queued artifact
- Do not skip ahead
- Do not draft multiple artifacts
- Do not introduce unfrozen structures without marking them provisional

CURRENT NEXT ARTIFACT  
continuation_integrity_protocol.md

AFTER THAT ORDER

1. continuation_preflight_checklist.md
2. bootstrap_prompt.lossless_continuation.md
3. controller_contract.yaml
4. phase_contract.yaml
5. artifact_contract.yaml

START NOW.  
First perform Continuation Integrity Check.  
Do not draft any contract before that check passes.

---

## Why This Fixes the Problem

The earlier continuation prompt failed because it said, in effect:

- here is the canon
- now begin drafting

That still leaves room for the new workspace to:

- compress distinctions
- rename stages
- infer structures
- widen scope

The fixed version changes the order to:

- here is the canon
- here is the anti-drift protocol
- prove you understood it exactly
- only then draft one artifact

That is the correct control model for Maestro.

---

## Additional Hardening I Recommend

Add these explicit laws to the bootstrap:

### Handoff Safety Law

- No frozen distinction may be paraphrased if paraphrase weakens operational specificity.

### Phase Fidelity Law

- Phase names with different operational consequences must remain textually distinct.

### Freeze Fidelity Law

- Draft freeze, definitive lock, and surface freeze may never be merged into one generic freeze.

### Taxonomy Restraint Law

- Where the canon specifies role coverage but not named councils, do not invent council names.

### Scope Restraint Law

- Controller contracts should bind only to currently frozen address scope; downstream granular address expansion belongs in later artifact layers.

---

## Best Way to Proceed now

I would not resume normal artifact drafting immediately.

I would do this in the next workspace:

1. paste `SESSION SNAPSHOT`
2. paste `CONTINUATION INTEGRITY PROTOCOL`
3. paste `BOOTSTRAP_EXECUTION_PROMPT`

Then let the model produce:

- `## Continuation Integrity Check`
- `## Continuation Verdict`

Only if it passes should it draft:

- `continuation_integrity_protocol.md`

After that, proceed to:

- `continuation_preflight_checklist.md`
- `bootstrap_prompt.lossless_continuation.md`
- `controller_contract.yaml`

## My Recommendation

The old continuation script should be retired completely.

The new standard should be:

- **snapshot**
- **protocol**
- **execution bootstrap**
- **preflight**
- **single-artifact drafting**
