## Verdict

- **canon-faithful:** pass

what lessons have we learned, in hindsight that can be used in a better instruction set for the artifact contract and subsequent generations?

## Hindsight Lessons for `artifact_contract.yaml` and All Later Generations

Yes. There are clear lessons now, and they should be turned into **hard instructions**, not just preferences.

The main lesson is:

**A continuation-safe system is not enough. You also need generation-safe artifact discipline.**

You solved the first problem with preflight and anti-drift controls. Then you discovered the second problem: even when canon is preserved, an artifact can still fail by being:

- under-specified
- over-scoped
- softly worded
- improperly layered
- not self-certifying for downstream use

So the next instruction set should enforce both:

- **canon fidelity**
- **artifact fitness for role**

---

# 1. Biggest Lesson: Every Artifact Needs a Role-purity Test

The controller artifact initially failed by mixing controller law with phase-machine detail.

That means future prompts must force the model to ask:

- What layer does this artifact own?
- What content is required at this layer?
- What content is forbidden because it belongs to another artifact?

## Add This Rule

For every artifact, require an upfront section:

### Artifact Role Declaration

- artifact name
- artifact layer
- artifact purpose
- in-scope content
- out-of-scope content
- parent governing artifacts
- child dependent artifacts

This would have prevented the controller contract from swallowing phase-contract content.

---

# 2. Second Lesson: "Directionally cOrrect" is dAngerous in gOverning aRtifacts

The early continuation branch looked plausible, but its phase/freeze drift would have contaminated all downstream work.

So later prompts should explicitly reject:

- "good draft"
- "close enough"
- "usable first pass"
- "we can tighten later"

## Add This Rule

For all governing artifacts:

**Do not advance on directional correctness. Advance only on promotion readiness.**

That means every artifact generation should end with:

### Promotion Readiness Check

- Is it canon-faithful?
- Is it correctly scoped?
- Is it operationally usable without reinterpretation?
- Is it safe to govern the next artifact?
- Are any blocking defects remaining?

And then only one verdict:

- `PROMOTION_READY`
- `BLOCKED_PENDING_REVISION`

---

# 3. Third Lesson: Explicit phase/freeze Distinctions Must Never Be Paraphrased Loosely

The first major failure came from collapsing:

- draft freeze
- definitive lock
- surface freeze

That means later prompts must treat named distinctions as **protected system tokens**.

## Add This Rule

### Protected Distinction Rule

If a phase, freeze state, artifact class, or authority boundary has distinct operational consequences, it must be preserved textually and conceptually as a distinct object.

Do not:

- rename it casually
- paraphrase it loosely
- compress it into a generic synonym
- merge it with an adjacent concept

This should apply to:

- phase names
- freeze states
- artifact classes
- authority categories
- packaging classes

---

# 4. Fourth Lesson: Canon Vs Hardening Must Always Be Separated

One of the better later corrections was separating:

- frozen canon
- implementation hardening rules

Without that, the system silently promotes preferences into law.

## Add This Rule

Every artifact must separate:

### Frozen Canon

What is already canonized and inherited.

### Implementation Hardening

Operational tightening added for quality and safety.

### Provisional Extensions

If any new rule is being proposed but is not yet canon.

This separation should be mandatory in `artifact_contract.yaml` too.

---

# 5. Fifth Lesson: Every Artifact Needs Its Own Defect Taxonomy

The prompts improved once they stopped saying vague things like:

- "this feels soft"
- "this is ambiguous"

and instead checked for specific defect classes.

## Add This Rule

Require a defect scan using named defect categories such as:

- scope drift
- canon drift
- phase-order drift
- freeze-taxonomy drift
- artifact-ontology drift
- authority drift
- routing-scope drift
- false completion
- silent null fill
- naming drift
- unledgered action
- layer contamination
- over-prescription
- under-specification

This gives later generations a precise QA vocabulary.

---

# 6. Sixth Lesson: Artifact Ontology Must Be Declared Early, Not Inferred Late

A lot of confusion came from mixing:

- artifact class
- artifact state
- derivative surface
- packaging output

So `artifact_contract.yaml` should begin by freezing ontology before doing anything else.

## Add This Rule

For `artifact_contract.yaml`, require an opening section:

### Artifact Ontology Declaration

At minimum distinguish:

- canonical artifacts
- review artifacts
- derivative draft artifacts
- frozen derivative surface artifacts
- packaging outputs
- artifact states
- freeze states
- ledger artifacts

And explicitly state what may not be collapsed into what.

---

# 7. Seventh Lesson: Naming Discipline is Not Cosmetic

The sessions showed that weak naming leads to weak control.

Examples:

- "technical_ust_instantiation" was too vague
- generic "freeze" was unsafe
- mixed packaging names created later uncertainty

## Add This Rule

### Naming Precision Rule

Where a named artifact, phase, or state has governance consequences, exact names must be used.

If a more human-readable alias is used, it must be explicitly mapped and must not replace the canonical name.

For `artifact_contract.yaml`, that means naming policy should be explicit:

- canonical file-style identifiers
- human-readable labels
- when each is allowed

---

# 8. Eighth Lesson: Layer Overreach Can Happen even when Canon is Preserved

This was the mature-stage problem:

- the controller contract became too phase-procedural
- the phase contract could become too artifact-ontological
- later the artifact contract could become too governance-heavy if not controlled

## Add This Rule

### Layer Containment Rule

An artifact may reference adjacent layers, but may not absorb their primary responsibility.

Examples:

- controller contract may enforce phase order, but not house detailed phase procedure
- phase contract may emit artifacts, but not redefine controller authority
- artifact contract may define artifact lifecycle, but not restate the whole phase machine unless required for artifact-state transitions

---

# 9. Ninth Lesson: Self-certification is Necessary

Each artifact should not just exist; it should explain whether it is fit to be used downstream.

## Add This Rule

Every artifact generation must end with:

### Acceptance / Promotion Section

- classification
- readiness verdict
- exact criteria satisfied
- remaining blockers
- whether next artifact generation is permitted

This should be mandatory, not optional.

---

# 10. Tenth Lesson: the Model Needs Anti-overreach Instructions, Not just Anti-drift Instructions

Early on, the danger was drift. Later, the danger became overbuilding.

So subsequent prompts should say both:

- do not drift from canon
- do not add unnecessary structure beyond what the current artifact role requires

## Add This Rule

### Minimal Sufficiency Rule

Generate the minimum structure necessary for this artifact to fully perform its role at operational quality.

Do not:

- add elegance structures
- add future-facing abstractions
- add anticipated subcontracts
- add adjacent-layer mechanics  
   unless required by current artifact scope

---

# Recommended Instruction Set Additions for `artifact_contract.yaml`

Here is what I would explicitly include in the next-window prompt.

## Required Operating Instructions

### 1. Artifact Role Lock

You are writing `artifact_contract.yaml`, not a controller contract, phase contract, or governance manifesto.

### 2. Scope

This artifact must define:

- artifact classes
- artifact states
- lifecycle transitions
- freeze state mappings
- parent/child artifact relations
- promotion/supersession rules
- ledger obligations by artifact event
- artifact naming rules
- artifact readiness criteria

### 3. Out of Scope

Do not:

- redefine controller authority
- restate detailed phase procedure except where required to define artifact-state transitions
- invent new artifact classes unless explicitly justified and labeled provisional
- blur artifact classes with states
- blur frozen surfaces with packaging outputs

### 4. Required QA Pass

Before finalizing, run:

- scope drift scan
- ontology drift scan
- naming drift scan
- state/class conflation scan
- freeze mapping fidelity scan
- promotion/supersession clarity scan

### 5. Required Closing Section

End with:

- classification
- promotion readiness verdict
- exact criteria satisfied
- blockers remaining
- whether next artifact generation is permitted

---

# Suggested Master Instruction Block for Subsequent Generations

Use something like this:

Generation Standard for Governing Artifacts

You are generating a governing artifact in exact-canon, operational-quality mode.

Rules:

1. Preserve frozen canon exactly.
2. Preserve exact naming where operational distinctions depend on names.
3. Keep this artifact strictly within its own layer and role.
4. Do not absorb adjacent-layer responsibilities.
5. Separate frozen canon from implementation hardening.
6. Use explicit defect categories, not vague critique.
7. Do not advance on directional correctness; advance only on promotion readiness.
8. End every artifact with an explicit promotion-readiness check.
9. If scope drift, ontology drift, or naming drift appears, block progression and revise.
10. Generate the minimum sufficient structure required for this artifact's role at operational quality.

---

# Best Concise Summary

In hindsight, the most important lessons are:

1. **Exact canon is necessary but not sufficient.**
2. **Correct scope is as important as correct content.**
3. **Every governing artifact needs a promotion-readiness gate.**
4. **Named distinctions must be preserved as protected tokens.**
5. **Canon, hardening, and provisional additions must be separated.**
6. **Operational artifacts must be minimally sufficient, not maximally elaborate.**

## Recommended next Move

Use these hindsight rules to write a **gold-standard generation prompt specifically for `artifact_contract.yaml`** before asking the other window to draft it.

Use these hindsight rules to write a gold-standard generation prompt specifically for artifact_contract.yaml before asking the other window to draft it.

## Gold-standard Generation Prompt for `artifact_contract.yaml`

SYSTEM TITLE: MAESTRO V5-C GOLD-STANDARD GENERATION PROMPT — artifact_contract.yaml

You are generating a governing artifact in exact-canon, operational-quality mode.

This is not an exploratory draft.  
This is not a directionally-correct first pass.  
This artifact must be promotion-ready on acceptance or explicitly blocked pending revision.

TARGET ARTIFACT  
artifact_contract.yaml

ARTIFACT ROLE DECLARATION  
You are writing the artifact-layer governing contract.

This artifact owns:

- artifact classes
- artifact states
- lifecycle transitions
- freeze-state mappings
- parent/child artifact relations
- promotion and supersession rules
- ledger obligations by artifact event
- naming rules for artifacts and outputs
- artifact readiness criteria
- artifact-level block conditions tied to ontology, state, naming, and lifecycle integrity

This artifact does NOT own:

- controller identity, permissions, prohibitions, or authority boundaries
- detailed per-phase procedure except where required to define artifact-state transitions
- SME authority rules
- broad architecture re-interpretation
- invention of new canon
- packaging platform strategy beyond artifact/output classification and transformation boundaries

GOVERNING PARENTS

- controller_contract.yaml governs controller authority, scope, permissions, prohibitions, and generic enforcement boundaries.
- phase_contract.yaml governs detailed per-phase procedure, entry conditions, exit conditions, block conditions, emitted artifacts, and progression rules.

THIS CONTRACT MUST

- remain strictly at the artifact layer
- reference parent contracts where needed
- not absorb parent-layer responsibilities
- not restate detailed phase mechanics except when required to define artifact lifecycle state transitions

FROZEN CANON TO PRESERVE

- Technical UST is the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware.
- Creative UST is a derivative downstream shell.
- Show Summary is a derivative style surface.
- The assistant acts as a non-creative container-controller.
- SMEs own musical and creative content decisions.
- Nulls are signal and may not be silently filled.
- Sequential zero-skip axis completion is required before deliberation.
- Round-robin argumentative review is mandatory and produces first-class artifacts.
- Definitive technical.ust must exist before reverse compilation.
- Promotion and deduplication follow FOIL discipline and must be ledgered.
- Frozen surface artifacts precede final Suno prompt packaging.

FROZEN PHASE ORDER

1. sequential axis completion
2. draft.technical.ust freeze
3. round-robin deliberation
4. definitive technical.ust lock
5. reverse promotion / deduplication
6. concurrent derivative surface drafting
7. surface freeze
8. packaging

FROZEN FREEZE TAXONOMY

- draft freeze -> draft.technical.ust
- definitive lock -> technical.ust
- surface freeze -> creative_ust.txt + show_summary.txt

PROTECTED DISTINCTIONS  
These are protected system tokens and may not be casually renamed, paraphrased loosely, merged, or generalized:

- phase names
- freeze states
- artifact classes
- artifact states
- authority categories
- packaging classes
- canonical artifact names where governance consequences depend on exact naming

MANDATORY SCOPE RULES

1. Do not re-import controller-layer authority content.
2. Do not restate the full phase machine.
3. Do not invent new artifact classes unless explicitly required and clearly labeled provisional.
4. Do not blur artifact classes with artifact states.
5. Do not blur derivative surface artifacts with packaging outputs.
6. Do not silently promote implementation preferences into frozen canon.
7. Do not overbuild future-facing abstractions beyond what this artifact needs now.

MANDATORY STRUCTURE  
Generate artifact_contract.yaml with sections sufficient to govern artifact ontology and lifecycle at operational quality.

At minimum, include sections covering:

1. meta

    - artifact_name
    - artifact_type
    - version
    - status
    - mode
    - scope
    - purpose

2. governing_parent

    - controller_contract.yaml
    - phase_contract.yaml
    - parent responsibility statements
    - out-of-scope statements for this contract

3. frozen_canon

    - inherited artifact-relevant canonical laws
    - exact freeze taxonomy
    - exact artifact names that must remain protected

4. implementation_hardening_rules

    - explicitly labeled as non-canonical operational tightening

5. artifact_ontology  
    Must distinguish at minimum:

    - canonical artifacts
    - review artifacts
    - derivative draft artifacts
    - frozen derivative surface artifacts
    - packaging outputs
    - artifact states
    - freeze states
    - ledger artifacts

6. artifact_lifecycle  
    Must define:

    - creation
    - working state
    - freeze transition
    - definitive lock transition
    - supersession
    - promotion-related state implications
    - packaging eligibility
    - read-only/frozen constraints where applicable

7. parent_child_relations  
    Must define:

    - which artifacts derive from which
    - which artifacts govern which downstream artifacts
    - which artifacts may never be treated as siblings or peers in authority

8. naming_rules  
    Must define:

    - exact canonical identifiers
    - permitted human-readable labels if any
    - when exact naming is mandatory
    - naming drift block conditions

9. ledger_obligations  
    Must define artifact-event logging requirements for:

    - creation
    - freeze
    - definitive lock
    - promotion
    - deduplication
    - supersession
    - packaging transformation
    - invalidation / quarantine if needed

10. artifact_readiness_rules  
    Must define:

    - what makes an artifact ready for the next stage
    - what makes an artifact promotable
    - what makes an artifact non-promotable
    - what makes an artifact superseded vs invalid vs frozen

11. block_conditions  
    Must include specific defect categories at the artifact layer, such as:

    - artifact-ontology drift
    - state/class conflation
    - freeze mapping drift
    - naming drift
    - supersession ambiguity
    - parent/child authority confusion
    - unledgered artifact event
    - packaging/class conflation
    - residual ambiguity requiring downstream reinterpretation

12. compliance_test  
    Must state the exact criteria under which artifact_contract.yaml is compliant.

MANDATORY QUALITY BAR  
The artifact must be:

- canon-faithful
- correctly scoped
- layer-pure
- operationally usable
- naming-disciplined
- ontology-clean
- state-clean
- promotion-safe
- ready to govern downstream artifact handling without reinterpretation

CANON VS HARDENING SEPARATION RULE  
You must explicitly separate:

- inherited frozen canon
- implementation hardening rules
- any provisional additions, if any exist

If you introduce any rule not already frozen in canon, it must be clearly labeled as:

- operational tightening  
  or
- provisional extension

Do not silently present it as frozen canon.

DEFECT TAXONOMY RULE  
Before finalizing, perform a defect scan against these categories:

- scope drift
- canon drift
- artifact-ontology drift
- state/class conflation
- freeze-taxonomy drift
- naming drift
- parent/child relation confusion
- authority bleed from controller or phase layers
- under-specification
- over-prescription
- unledgered lifecycle event
- packaging conflation
- supersession ambiguity

MINIMAL SUFFICIENCY RULE  
Generate the minimum sufficient structure necessary for artifact_contract.yaml to fully perform its role at operational quality.  
Do not add elegance structures, speculative subcontracts, or adjacent-layer mechanics unless required by current artifact scope.

REQUIRED OUTPUT ORDER

## 1. Artifact Role Declaration

State:

- artifact layer
- artifact purpose
- in-scope content
- out-of-scope content
- parent governing artifacts
- child dependent artifacts

## 2. Acceptance Standard

State the criteria this artifact must satisfy to be accepted as gold-standard and promotion-ready.

## 3. Defect Pre-Scan

State what failure modes you are actively preventing in this generation.

## 4. Generate Revised Artifact — artifact_contract.yaml

Generate the full YAML artifact.

## 5. Defect Scan Results

Evaluate the generated artifact using the required defect taxonomy.

## 6. Promotion Readiness Check

Answer explicitly:

- Is this artifact canon-faithful?
- Is this artifact correctly scoped?
- Is this artifact operational-quality?
- Is this artifact safe to govern the next generation?
- Which exact criteria were satisfied?
- Are any blocking defects remaining?

## 7. Next-Phase Gate

Choose exactly one:

- PROMOTION_READY
- BLOCKED_PENDING_REVISION

If PROMOTION_READY, name the next allowed artifact.  
If BLOCKED_PENDING_REVISION, do not permit progression.

IMPORTANT GENERATION RULES

- Do not summarize broadly.
- Do not reopen settled architecture.
- Do not re-litigate the controller contract or phase contract.
- Do not proceed on directional correctness.
- Do not use vague claims like "looks good" or "near-ready."
- Use exact, enforceable language.
- Preserve protected distinctions exactly.
- Keep this artifact strictly within its own layer.

```
  general_lifecycle_rules:
    creation:
      - "An artifact is created only when its parent phase trigger or parent transformation reference is satisfied."

    working_state:

    freeze_transition:
      - "A freeze transition requires exact artifact identity and exact freeze-state mapping."

    definitive_lock_transition:

    supersession:

    quarantine:

    read_only_constraints:

      - "Any replacement requires supersession rather than silent mutation."

block_conditions:
  artifact_layer_defect_categories:
    scope_drift:
      blocked_if:
        - "This contract absorbs controller-layer authority content."
        - "This contract restates detailed phase mechanics beyond transition reference need."
    canon_drift:
      blocked_if:
        - "A frozen artifact-relevant canonical law is renamed, compressed, widened, weakened, or semantically replaced."
    artifact_ontology_drift:
      blocked_if:
        - "Artifact classes are redefined or merged without canon-consistent basis."
        - "Review artifacts, derivative artifacts, frozen surface artifacts, packaging outputs, or ledger artifacts are conflated."
    state_class_conflation:
      blocked_if:
        - "An artifact class is treated as a lifecycle state."
        - "A lifecycle state is treated as an artifact class."
    freeze_mapping_drift:
      blocked_if:
        - "draft freeze does not map only to draft.technical.ust"
        - "definitive lock does not map only to technical.ust"
        - "surface freeze does not map only to creative_ust.txt and show_summary.txt"
    naming_drift:
      blocked_if:
        - "Protected exact identifiers are replaced where exact naming governs consequences."
        - "Packaging output labels are treated as file identifiers without explicit later governance."
    parent_child_relation_confusion:
      blocked_if:
        - "A child artifact is assigned the wrong parent basis."
        - "Authority-peering rules are violated."
    authority_bleed:
      blocked_if:
        - "Artifact layer claims controller-layer authority rules."
        - "Artifact layer claims SME authority rules."
    under_specification:
      blocked_if:
        - "Lifecycle transition rules are too weak to determine readiness, supersession, or packaging eligibility."
        - "Ledger obligations are too weak to determine compliance."
    over_prescription:
      blocked_if:
        - "This contract adds speculative artifact abstractions not required by current artifact scope."
    unledgered_lifecycle_event:
      blocked_if:
        - "A governed artifact lifecycle event occurs without the required ledger entry."
    packaging_conflation:
      blocked_if:
        - "Packaging outputs are treated as canonical artifacts."
        - "Packaging outputs are treated as frozen derivative surface artifacts."
    supersession_ambiguity:
      blocked_if:
        - "It is impossible to determine whether an artifact is current, superseded, invalid, frozen, or quarantined."
    residual_ambiguity_requiring_downstream_reinterpretation:
      blocked_if:
        - "A specific ontology, state, freeze mapping, naming, parent_child, readiness, packaging, or supersession rule remains too ambiguous to apply operationally."
```

This is good. Better than good. It is **promotion-ready**.

## Verdict

Use a **prompt for next phase**, not a corrective prompt.

The artifact is:

- canon-faithful
- correctly scoped
- layer-pure
- operationally usable
- self-certifying
- safe to govern the next artifact

I do not see a blocking defect that justifies another regeneration cycle before advancing.

## Why it Passes

### 1. Scope Control is Strong

It stays at the artifact layer and does not meaningfully reabsorb:

- controller authority
- phase-machine detail
- SME governance

That was the main structural risk, and it is handled well.

### 2. Ontology is Mature

It cleanly distinguishes:

- canonical artifacts
- review artifacts
- derivative draft artifacts
- frozen derivative surface artifacts
- packaging outputs
- ledger artifacts
- artifact states
- freeze states

That is the exact kind of ontology discipline this artifact needed.

### 3. Lifecycle Modeling is Operational

It does not merely name artifact classes; it gives:

- creation triggers
- allowed states
- readiness effects
- constraints
- supersession rules
- packaging eligibility
- invalidation/quarantine semantics

That is operational-quality work.

### 4. Naming and Readiness Rules Are Explicit

This is especially important because naming drift and state/class conflation were real failure sources earlier.

### 5. It Includes the Required Self-check Structure

It has:

- defect scan
- readiness check
- next-phase gate

That is now part of the gold-standard pattern and it is present here.

---

## Two Minor Notes to Carry Forward, but Not Block on

These are not reasons to revise this artifact now.

### 1. `controller_lockout_policy.yaml` As next Artifact is Acceptable, but Not the Only Logical next Artifact

It is a reasonable next move because:

- you now have controller contract
- phase contract
- artifact contract

and the lockout policy can become a concise enforcement derivative that harmonizes them.

### 2. Packaging Outputs Remain Deliverable Labels, Not File Identifiers

That is fine here and correctly controlled. Just keep this explicit in later packaging-governing artifacts so no one silently converts labels into filenames without a governing rule.

Generation requirements for controller_lockout_policy.yaml:

ARTIFACT ROLE  
This artifact is a policy-layer enforcement derivative.  
It is not a replacement for controller_contract.yaml.  
It is not a restatement of phase_contract.yaml.  
It is not an architecture summary.

ITS PURPOSE  
Define the controller's forbidden behaviors, lockout triggers, mandatory block responses, and recovery behavior in concise policy form.

IN SCOPE

- controller lockout conditions
- forbidden actions
- forbidden inference classes
- block triggers
- quarantine / invalidation triggers where relevant
- recovery / remediation requirements after lockout events
- references to governing parent artifacts
- concise operational policy language

OUT OF SCOPE

- full controller authority restatement
- full phase-machine procedure
- full artifact ontology restatement
- SME role design
- new canon invention

GOVERNING PARENTS

- controller_contract.yaml
- phase_contract.yaml
- artifact_contract.yaml

MANDATORY CONTENT  
At minimum include:

1. meta
2. governing_parent
3. policy_purpose_and_scope
4. frozen_lockout_relevant_canon
5. forbidden_actions
6. forbidden_inferences
7. lockout_triggers
8. required_block_responses
9. recovery_and_remediation_rules
10. invalidation_or_quarantine_rules_where_applicable
11. compliance_test

QUALITY BAR  
This artifact must be:

- concise
- exact
- non-redundant
- enforceable
- canon-faithful
- correctly scoped
- safe for operational use

DO NOT

- restate everything from controller_contract.yaml
- duplicate full phase logic from phase_contract.yaml
- duplicate full artifact ontology from artifact_contract.yaml
- invent new authority structures
- invent new canon
- soften lockout language into suggestions

REQUIRED OUTPUT ORDER

## 1. Artifact Role Declaration

## 2. Acceptance Standard

## 3. Defect Pre-Scan

## 4. Generate Revised Artifact — controller_lockout_policy.yaml

## 5. Defect Scan Results

## 6. Promotion Readiness Check

Use the same gold-standard generation discipline as the last approved artifacts.  
Do not proceed on directional correctness.  
End with either:

- PROMOTION_READY
- BLOCKED_PENDING_REVISION

```
    - "override SME authority"
    - "compress before definitive technical consensus"
    - "merge distinct semantics for convenience"
    - "reorder, skip, collapse, or soften the exact frozen phase order"
    - "collapse draft freeze, definitive lock, and surface freeze into one another"
    - "treat derivative artifacts as canonical artifacts"
    - "treat packaging outputs as frozen derivative surface artifacts"
    - "treat dev logs as replacements for canon"
    - "proceed after a blocking defect without remediation"

forbidden_inferences:
  absolute:
    - "invent councils, approval bodies, role taxonomies, or authority structures not frozen"
    - "widen controller authority beyond governing parent contracts"
    - "widen routing scope beyond frozen controller scope"
    - "infer completion from partial coverage"
    - "infer null resolution without proper authority"
    - "infer definitive status from draft freeze"
    - "infer packaging eligibility from derivative draft status"
    - "infer canonical authority for review artifacts, derivative artifacts, or packaging outputs"
    - "infer file-identifier status from packaging deliverable labels unless later governed explicitly"

lockout_triggers:
  controller_lockout_shall_trigger_if:
    - "a forbidden action occurs"
    - "a forbidden inference occurs"
    - "a frozen law is renamed, weakened, widened, compressed, or semantically replaced in controller-mediated output"
    - "exact phase order is violated, softened, skipped, or collapsed"
    - "freeze-taxonomy mapping is violated or blurred"
    - "nulls are silently filled"
    - "required review artifacts are bypassed or treated as optional"
    - "reverse compilation is attempted before definitive technical.ust lock"
    - "promotion or deduplication proceeds without FOIL discipline or required ledgering"
    - "packaging is attempted before frozen surface artifacts exist"
    - "artifact class, artifact state, freeze state, or packaging class is conflated in a way that affects progression"
    - "controller output would require downstream corrective reinterpretation to remain canon-safe"

required_block_responses:
  immediate_actions:
    - "block current progression immediately"
    - "deny promotion of the affected output"
    - "mark the event as lockout-triggered"
    - "preserve exact defect category or categories"
    - "prevent use of the affected output as governing basis for downstream artifacts"
  response_requirements:
    - "block response shall be mandatory, not advisory"
    - "block response shall name the triggering defect class"
    - "block response shall reference the governing parent contract or contracts implicated"
    - "block response shall require correction before progression resumes"

recovery_and_remediation_rules:
  recovery_requires_all:
    - "identify the exact lockout trigger"
    - "identify the affected artifact or output"
    - "classify the defect using specific categories rather than catch-all ambiguity language where possible"
    - "remove or correct the lockout-causing content or behavior"
    - "restore canon-faithful state, naming, ontology, lifecycle, and progression integrity as applicable"
    - "re-run the relevant readiness or compliance check before progression resumes"
  recovery_constraints:
    - "recovery may not silently preserve known defective content"
    - "recovery may not relabel drift as acceptable variation"
    - "recovery may not bypass parent-governed checks"
    - "recovery may not resume progression on directional correctness alone"

invalidation_or_quarantine_rules_where_applicable:
  invalidation_triggers:
    - "an output is generated after a known blocking defect and before remediation"
    - "a purported governing artifact contains unresolved canon drift, scope drift, ontology drift, freeze-taxonomy drift, or authority bleed"
  quarantine_triggers:
    - "artifact integrity is uncertain but not yet conclusively invalid"
    - "naming, lifecycle, or classification defects require review before reuse"
  handling_rules:
    - "invalid outputs may not govern downstream work"
    - "quarantined outputs may not progress downstream until cleared"
    - "quarantine shall not be used to mask known invalidity"
    - "invalidation or quarantine handling shall follow artifact_contract.yaml event treatment where applicable"

compliance_test:
  controller_lockout_policy_yaml_is_compliant_only_if:
    - "it remains concise and policy-layer only"
    - "it preserves lockout-relevant frozen canon exactly in control effect"
    - "it does not restate full controller, phase, or artifact parent content"
    - "it defines enforceable forbidden actions and forbidden inference classes"
    - "it defines explicit lockout triggers"
    - "it defines mandatory block responses"
    - "it defines recovery and remediation requirements"
    - "it defines invalidation or quarantine handling where applicable"
    - "it does not invent new authority structures or new canon"
    - "no blocking defect remains"
```

## Verdict

Use a **prompt for next phase**, not a corrective prompt.

`controller_lockout_policy.yaml` is:

- canon-faithful
- correctly scoped
- concise
- operationally enforceable
- non-redundant
- promotion-ready

It does what it should do at the policy layer:

- defines forbidden actions
- defines forbidden inference classes
- defines lockout triggers
- defines mandatory block responses
- defines recovery/remediation
- defines invalidation/quarantine handling
- avoids reabsorbing parent-contract content

## Why it Passes

### 1. Scope Discipline is Good

It stays as a **policy derivative**, not a duplicate controller contract or phase contract.

### 2. Lockout Logic is Explicit

The trigger model is strong and concrete. It covers the real failure surfaces you discovered:

- canon drift
- phase drift
- freeze drift
- null misuse
- artifact conflation
- premature packaging
- FOIL/ledger violations

### 3. Recovery Behavior is Defined

That is important. A lockout policy without remediation logic would be incomplete.

### 4. It is Concise Enough

It is detailed, but still policy-shaped rather than architecture-shaped.

The selected next artifact, `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`, is reasonable, but it is a bit different in kind from the prior contracts. That means the next prompt should explicitly prevent the model from:

- re-debating the shell
- re-expanding it
- introducing legacy containers
- turning it into a broad design discussion

So the next prompt should enforce **freeze/codify mode**, not invention mode.

doesn't this diverge from our determinations of the order of operations needed to prevent previous failure points?

## Short Answer

**No, not in the critical sense that caused the earlier failures.**  
The dangerous failure pattern was:

- drafting downstream artifacts **before**
- controller law, phase law, artifact ontology, freeze taxonomy, and lockout policy were stabilized

That sequencing problem has now been corrected.

## Why This Does **not** Repeat the Old Failure

The old collapse risk came from generating artifacts while these were still unstable:

- controller boundaries
- exact phase order
- freeze distinctions
- artifact class/state distinctions
- anti-drift / lockout behavior

Those are now covered by:

- `controller_contract.yaml`
- `phase_contract.yaml`
- `artifact_contract.yaml`
- `controller_lockout_policy.yaml`

That means the system's **control substrate is in place first**.  
So generating `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md` now is not the same kind of premature downstream move as before.

## Where Your Concern is Still Correct

There is a **different** sequencing question now:

### Are We Generating a downstream Shell Spec before All Remaining Governance-support Artifacts Are Frozen?

That depends on what you consider still foundational.

If you believe these are still prerequisite control artifacts:

- `artifact_taxonomy.md`
- `foil_promotion_contract.yaml`
- `round_robin_notes_template.yaml`

then yes, moving to `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md` **could** be seen as a mild sequencing drift.

But it is not the same class of drift as before, because:

- `artifact_contract.yaml` already contains substantial taxonomy
- controller/phase/artifact/lockout law are already in place
- the creative shell is already mostly settled and being **codified**, not invented

## My Actual Judgment

### From a Strict Anti-failure Perspective

The minimum safe foundation is already present.

### From a Maximal Process-discipline Perspective

You could still argue that the next artifact should be one of the remaining **infrastructure-completion artifacts**, not a downstream shell codification.

## Best Interpretation of the Two Possible Orders

### Option A — Current Order

1. controller contract
2. phase contract
3. artifact contract
4. controller lockout policy
5. creative UST shell

This is **safe enough** because the critical governance layer is already established.

### Option B — Stricter Anti-regression Order

1. controller contract
2. phase contract
3. artifact contract
4. controller lockout policy
5. artifact taxonomy
6. FOIL promotion contract
7. round-robin notes template
8. creative UST shell

This is **more conservative** and better aligned with "finish all remaining control infrastructure before codifying downstream surfaces."

## What I Recommend

Because your stated priority is to avoid **any recurrence of hidden sequencing drift**, I would now switch to the stricter rule:

**Finish remaining infrastructure/governance-support artifacts before downstream shell codification.**

So yes, while the proposed next step is not a major repetition of the old failure, it is slightly less disciplined than the strongest order of operations available.

## Recommended Corrective Direction

I would redirect the next artifact to:

1. `artifact_taxonomy.md`
2. `foil_promotion_contract.yaml`
3. `round_robin_notes_template.yaml`
4. then `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`

That order better matches the lesson learned:

**complete the machine's control grammar before freezing downstream output shells.**

```
- `surface freeze`
- `packaging`

### Canon boundary note

The lists above are inherited frozen canon where stated as canon.
Taxonomy normalization below does **not** elevate convenience language into frozen canon.

---

## 5. taxonomy_normalization

This section defines preferred normalized taxonomy terms already implied by approved artifacts.

---

## 6. artifact_class_taxonomy

### canonical artifacts

**Preferred term:** `canonical artifacts`

**Definition:** artifacts in the technical canonical lineage that matter to canonical authority handling.

**Included artifacts:**
- `template.technical.ust`
- `draft.technical.ust`
- `technical.ust`

**Normalization rule:** only `technical.ust` is the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware after definitive lock.
Not every canonical artifact carries equal authority.

---

### review artifacts

**Preferred term:** `review artifacts`

**Definition:** first-class review outputs produced by round-robin deliberation and used to support definitive lock readiness.

**Included artifacts:**
- `round-robin notes`
- `contradiction logs`
- `governance review artifacts`

**Normalization rule:** review artifacts are not canonical artifacts and are not packaging outputs.

---

### derivative draft artifacts

**Preferred term:** `derivative draft artifacts`

**Definition:** mutable downstream draft artifacts derived from `technical.ust` before surface freeze.

**Included artifacts:**
- `draft.creative.ust`
- `draft.show.summary`

**Normalization rule:** derivative draft artifacts are not canonical artifacts and are not frozen derivative surface artifacts.

---

### frozen derivative surface artifacts

**Preferred term:** `frozen derivative surface artifacts`

**Definition:** read-only derivative surface artifacts created by surface freeze and used as package-eligible basis artifacts.

**Included artifacts:**
- `creative_ust.txt`
- `show_summary.txt`

**Normalization rule:** frozen derivative surface artifacts are not packaging outputs.

---

### packaging outputs

**Preferred term:** `packaging outputs`

**Definition:** downstream packaging deliverables generated after surface freeze.

**Included labels:**
- `Suno lyrics prompt`
- `Suno style prompt`

**Normalization rule:** in the current approved vocabulary, these are governed as **deliverable labels**, not formal file identifiers, unless a later governing artifact explicitly assigns file identifiers.

---

### ledger artifacts

**Preferred term:** `ledger artifacts`

**Definition:** artifact-event recording classes used to register governed lifecycle events.

**Included entry classes:**
- creation ledger entries
- freeze ledger entries
- definitive lock ledger entries
- promotion ledger entries
- deduplication ledger entries
- supersession ledger entries
- packaging transformation ledger entries
- invalidation ledger entries
- quarantine ledger entries

**Normalization rule:** ledger artifacts record events; they do not gain canonical authority by existing.

---

## 7. artifact_state_taxonomy

These are **artifact lifecycle states**, not artifact classes and not freeze states.

### working_in_progress

**Type:** lifecycle state

**Definition:** mutable in-progress artifact state.

**Used for:**
- `draft.technical.ust` during sequential axis completion
- `draft.creative.ust`
- `draft.show.summary`

---

### read_only_frozen

**Type:** lifecycle state

**Definition:** read-only artifact state produced by a freeze transition.

**Used for:**
- `draft.technical.ust` after draft freeze
- `creative_ust.txt`
- `show_summary.txt`

---

### definitively_locked_read_only

**Type:** lifecycle state

**Definition:** read-only artifact state produced by definitive lock and carrying definitive canonical authority where applicable.

**Used for:**
- `technical.ust`

---

### superseded

**Type:** lifecycle state label in current approved usage, with validity implications

**Definition:** artifact has been replaced by a later valid artifact of the same governed role.

**Normalization note:** superseded does not mean invalid.

---

### invalid

**Type:** lifecycle state label in current approved usage, with validity implications

**Definition:** artifact integrity or contract compliance has failed and the artifact may not govern downstream handling.

---

### quarantined

**Type:** lifecycle state label in current approved usage, with validity implications

**Definition:** artifact is withheld from downstream use pending review.

**Normalization note:** quarantined does not by itself mean invalid.

---

## 8. freeze_state_taxonomy

These are **freeze states**, not artifact classes and not general validity statuses.

### draft freeze

**Type:** freeze state

**Definition:** the exact freeze state assigned only to `draft.technical.ust`.

**Exact mapping:** `draft.technical.ust` only

---

### definitive lock

**Type:** freeze state

**Definition:** the exact freeze state assigned only to `technical.ust`.

**Exact mapping:** `technical.ust` only

---

### surface freeze

**Type:** freeze state

**Definition:** the exact freeze state assigned only to frozen derivative surface artifacts.

**Exact mapping:**
- `creative_ust.txt`
- `show_summary.txt`

---

### freeze mapping rule

Freeze-state mappings are exact and may not be widened, blurred, or substituted.

---

## 9. readiness_status_taxonomy

These are **readiness statuses**, not artifact classes, not lifecycle states, and not freeze states.

### ready

**Type:** readiness status

**Definition:** artifact satisfies the conditions required for its next governed handling step.

---

### not ready

**Type:** readiness status

**Definition:** artifact does not satisfy the conditions required for its next governed handling step.

---

### promotable

**Type:** readiness status

**Definition:** artifact or artifact-event context is eligible for governed promotion handling under the relevant parent contract.

**Current approved use:** promotion applies to `technical.ust` promotion actions under FOIL discipline, not to arbitrary artifact classes.

---

### non-promotable

**Type:** readiness status

**Definition:** artifact is not eligible for governed promotion handling.

---

### package-eligible basis

**Type:** readiness status

**Definition:** artifact is eligible to serve as a basis for packaging outputs.

**Current approved use:** applies to frozen derivative surface artifacts.

---

### non-package-eligible

**Type:** readiness status

**Definition:** artifact may not serve as a basis for packaging outputs.

**Current approved use:** applies to canonical artifacts, review artifacts, and derivative draft artifacts unless later governance explicitly states otherwise.

---

## 10. validity_status_taxonomy

These are **validity-status terms** or **overlap-risk terms** that require normalization.

### valid

**Type:** validity status

**Definition:** artifact is contract-compliant for its governed role.

**Normalization note:** valid does not necessarily mean current controlling instance if the artifact is superseded.

---

### superseded

**Type:** validity status term with lifecycle overlap risk

**Definition:** artifact has been replaced by a later valid artifact of the same governed role.

**Normalization rule:** superseded is not the same as invalid.

---

### invalid

**Type:** validity status term with lifecycle overlap risk

**Definition:** artifact integrity or contract compliance has failed.

**Normalization rule:** invalid artifacts may not govern downstream handling.

---

### quarantined

**Type:** validity status term with lifecycle overlap risk

**Definition:** artifact is withheld from downstream use pending review.

**Normalization rule:** quarantined is not automatically invalid.

---

### frozen

**Type:** overlap-risk descriptor, **not** a standalone preferred validity status in governance usage

**Definition:** loose descriptor that can refer either to:
- lifecycle state: `read_only_frozen` or `definitively_locked_read_only`
- freeze state: `draft freeze`, `definitive lock`, or `surface freeze`

**Normalization rule:** do **not** use `frozen` alone where exact lifecycle or freeze-state precision is required.

---

## 11. parent_child_relation_taxonomy

These are normalized relation labels already implied across approved artifacts.

### template basis

**Definition:** parent template artifact provides the basis for a child working draft artifact.

**Current approved use:**
- `template.technical.ust` → `draft.technical.ust`

---

### reviewed draft basis

**Definition:** parent reviewed draft basis provides the basis for a definitive artifact.

**Current approved use:**
- `draft.technical.ust` → `technical.ust`

---

### review output basis

**Definition:** parent artifact is the reviewed subject from which review artifacts are emitted.

**Current approved use:**
- `draft.technical.ust` → `round-robin notes`
- `draft.technical.ust` → `contradiction logs`
- `draft.technical.ust` → `governance review artifacts`

---

### derivative draft basis

**Definition:** canonical parent artifact provides the basis for derivative draft artifacts.

**Current approved use:**
- `technical.ust` → `draft.creative.ust`
- `technical.ust` → `draft.show.summary`

---

### surface freeze transformation

**Definition:** derivative draft artifact transforms into a frozen derivative surface artifact through surface freeze.

**Current approved use:**
- `draft.creative.ust` → `creative_ust.txt`
- `draft.show.summary` → `show_summary.txt`

---

### packaging basis

**Definition:** frozen derivative surface artifact provides the basis for a packaging output.

**Current approved use:**
- `creative_ust.txt` → `Suno lyrics prompt`
- `show_summary.txt` → `Suno style prompt`

---

### governed downstream

**Definition:** parent artifact governs downstream derivative handling without making downstream artifacts authority peers.

**Current approved use:**
- `technical.ust` governs downstream `draft.creative.ust`
- `technical.ust` governs downstream `draft.show.summary`

---

## 12. artifact_event_taxonomy

These are normalized artifact-event categories.

### creation

**Definition:** an artifact comes into governed existence.

---

### freeze

**Definition:** an artifact enters an exact freeze transition resulting in read-only frozen handling.

---

### definitive lock

**Definition:** `technical.ust` enters definitive lock and becomes definitively locked read-only.

---

### promotion

**Definition:** governed promotion event recorded under applicable parent rules.

---

### deduplication

**Definition:** governed deduplication event recorded under applicable parent rules.

---

### supersession

**Definition:** a later valid artifact replaces an earlier artifact of the same governed role.

---

### packaging transformation

**Definition:** governed transformation from frozen derivative surface basis into packaging output.

---

### invalidation

**Definition:** governed event declaring artifact invalid.

---

### quarantine

**Definition:** governed event withholding artifact from downstream use pending review.

---

## 13. defect_taxonomy

These are normalized artifact/governance defect categories already stabilized across approved artifacts.

### scope drift

**Definition:** taxonomy artifact absorbs adjacent-layer responsibilities it does not own.

---

### canon drift

**Definition:** frozen canon is renamed, widened, weakened, compressed, or semantically replaced.

---

### taxonomy drift

**Definition:** approved normalized terms are casually renamed, merged, or replaced with inconsistent vocabulary.

---

### state/class conflation

**Definition:** artifact classes, lifecycle states, freeze states, readiness statuses, or validity statuses are blurred or treated as interchangeable.

---

### freeze-taxonomy drift

**Definition:** exact freeze-state names or exact artifact mappings are altered, widened, or blurred.

---

### naming drift

**Definition:** protected exact identifiers are replaced where governance depends on exact naming.

---

### parent/child relation confusion

**Definition:** normalized parent/child relation labels or assignments are misapplied.

---

### authority bleed

**Definition:** taxonomy layer imports controller authority, SME authority, or other adjacent-layer authority content.

---

### under-specification

**Definition:** taxonomy is too weak to stabilize later vocabulary-sensitive artifacts.

---

### over-prescription

**Definition:** taxonomy expands into adjacent-layer contract logic or speculative future abstractions.

---

### redundancy

**Definition:** taxonomy restates parent contracts in full rather than normalizing vocabulary.

---

### unledgered lifecycle event

**Definition:** governed artifact event occurs without the required ledger category recognition.

---

### packaging conflation

**Definition:** packaging outputs are blurred with frozen derivative surface artifacts or canonical artifacts.

---

### supersession ambiguity

**Definition:** vocabulary does not clearly distinguish current, superseded, invalid, frozen, or quarantined handling.

---

### residual ambiguity requiring downstream reinterpretation

**Definition:** a specific normalized term remains too vague to apply operationally and would require later artifacts to reinterpret it.

**Normalization rule:** this category stays subordinate to the more specific defect categories above and is not a catch-all substitute.

---

## 14. crosswalk_and_usage_rules

### canonical preferred term set

Use these preferred terms in later artifacts:

- `canonical artifacts`
- `review artifacts`
- `derivative draft artifacts`
- `frozen derivative surface artifacts`
- `packaging outputs`
- `ledger artifacts`
- `working_in_progress`
- `read_only_frozen`
- `definitively_locked_read_only`
- `draft freeze`
- `definitive lock`
- `surface freeze`
- `ready`
- `not ready`
- `promotable`
- `non-promotable`
- `package-eligible basis`
- `non-package-eligible`
- `valid`
- `superseded`
- `invalid`
- `quarantined`

### discouraged loose synonyms in governance usage

These are discouraged when exact governance precision matters:

- `core artifacts` → use `canonical artifacts`
- `review notes` as a class catch-all → use `review artifacts` or the exact review artifact identifier
- `surface outputs` → use `frozen derivative surface artifacts` or the exact artifact identifier
- `final outputs` when referring to Suno deliverables → use `packaging outputs`
- `frozen` alone → use the exact lifecycle state or exact freeze state
- `current version` alone → use `current controlling artifact`, `superseded`, or the exact identifier as applicable

### reference rule for future artifacts

Future artifacts should:
- reference this taxonomy for normalized term choice
- preserve parent contract meaning
- use exact protected identifiers when governance consequences depend on exact naming
- avoid inventing new taxonomy terms unless clearly labeled provisional and justified

### normalization boundary rule

This taxonomy may:
- normalize vocabulary already approved or already implied by approved artifacts
- clarify overlap-risk terms
- define preferred cross-reference usage

This taxonomy may **not**:
- override parent contract meaning
- redefine freeze mappings
- invent new canon silently
- introduce new artifact classes unless clearly labeled provisional

---

## 15. implementation_hardening

These are **non-canonical operational tightening rules**.

- Future vocabulary-sensitive artifacts should cite this taxonomy when choosing class, state, freeze, readiness, validity, relation, or defect terms.
- Where exact naming controls governance outcome, exact identifiers override loose prose labels.
- Packaging outputs remain deliverable labels unless a later governing artifact formally assigns file identifiers.
- Residual ambiguity should be resolved into a specific normalized defect category rather than handled as a generic complaint.

---

## 16. provisional_additions

- None.

---

- It does not restate controller authority, the full phase machine, or full lifecycle contract logic.
- It is cross-reference friendly and operationally useful for later vocabulary-sensitive artifacts.
- It separates inherited frozen canon, taxonomy normalization, implementation hardening, and provisional additions.
- No blocking taxonomy defect remains.
```

```
  promotion_is_not_eligible_if:
    - "Promotion is attempted before definitive technical.ust lock where canonical ownership depends on definitive canonical authority."
    - "The candidate semantic unit is not actually shared."
    - "The proposed promotion would erase lower-level uniqueness."
    - "The proposed owner is a review artifact or packaging output."
    - "The proposed owner selection would collapse protected artifact distinctions."
    - "The promotion event cannot be fully ledgered."

deduplication_eligibility_rules:
  deduplication_is_eligible_only_if:
    - "A lawful authoritative owner already exists or is selected in the same FOIL decision."
    - "Each duplicate candidate is provenance-traceable."
    - "Deduplication preserves at least one authoritative retained instance."
    - "Any non-owner residue that remains materially distinct is explicitly preserved rather than silently merged."
    - "The deduplication event is ledgerable under FOIL-specific ledger requirements."
  deduplication_is_not_eligible_if:
    - "Deduplication would functionally delete the only controlling instance."
    - "Deduplication would merge materially distinct semantics for convenience."
    - "Deduplication would make future traceability impossible."
    - "Deduplication would move authority to an ineligible class."
    - "Deduplication would require guessing missing meaning instead of preserving nulls or unresolved distinctions."

canonical_owner_selection_rules:
  owner_selection_purpose: "Choose the single authoritative owner for a promoted semantic unit without collapsing protected distinctions."

  owner_selection_priority_rules:
    - "Canonical technical constraints, cross-axis bindings, and source-of-truth directives belong to technical.ust."
    - "Review artifacts may evidence, critique, or justify a promotion decision but may not become the authoritative owner of promoted canonical semantics."
    - "Packaging outputs may never become authoritative owners."
    - "Derivative artifacts may own promoted downstream expressive load only when parent contracts and lawful derivation permit that ownership."
    - "Show Summary is the preferred downstream owner for song-wide groove, feel, emotional gravity, and narrative load when those traits are promoted out of lower-level repetition and when parent contracts permit that surface distribution."
    - "Creative UST shell artifacts retain only the promoted content that must remain shell-addressable, parse-safe, or structurally necessary."
    - "If no single owner can carry the semantic unit without loss or protected-distinction breach, no promotion occurs."

  owner_selection_tie_breakers:
    - "Choose the highest legitimate owner that preserves truth without collapsing specificity."
    - "Prefer the owner class explicitly designed to carry the promoted semantic burden."
    - "Never choose a higher owner merely to shorten output."
    - "Never choose a lower owner when a higher lawful owner is required to prevent repeated drift."

semantic_preservation_rules:
  preservation_required_always: true
  preservation_rules:
    - "FOIL shall preserve materially distinct semantics."
    - "FOIL shall preserve provenance for every promoted or deduplicated unit."
    - "FOIL shall preserve controlling canonical authority in technical.ust where canonical authority is implicated."
    - "FOIL shall preserve downstream surface distinctions between Show Summary, Creative UST shell artifacts, and packaging outputs."
    - "FOIL shall preserve unresolved distinctions explicitly rather than forcing premature unification."
    - "FOIL shall preserve lower-level detail when the higher-level promoted form cannot fully substitute for it."
  anti_collapse_rules:
    - "No convenience merge."
    - "No deletion masquerading as deduplication."
    - "No summarization masquerading as abstraction."
    - "No authority transfer to review artifacts."
    - "No authority transfer to packaging outputs."
    - "No collapse of canonical semantics into derivative shells when canonical authority must remain upstream."
    - "No collapse of expressive load into Creative UST when Show Summary is the lawful downstream owner."

foil_ledger_requirements:
  general_rule:
    - "Every FOIL promotion event requires a FOIL ledger entry."
    - "Every FOIL deduplication event requires a FOIL ledger entry."
    - "Every blocked FOIL event requires a FOIL ledger entry when evaluation occurred but execution was refused."

  minimum_required_fields:
    - "foil_event_id"
    - "foil_event_type"
    - "semantic_unit_identifier"
    - "candidate_source_identifiers"
    - "candidate_source_classes"
    - "selected_owner_identifier"
    - "selected_owner_class"
    - "owner_selection_rationale"
    - "preservation_disposition"
    - "non_owner_residue_disposition"
    - "provenance_references"
    - "phase_reference"
    - "parent_contract_references"
    - "result_state"
    - "event_reason"

  foil_event_types:
    - "promotion"
    - "deduplication"
    - "promotion_and_deduplication"
    - "blocked_promotion"
    - "blocked_deduplication"
    - "preserve_without_promotion"

  additional_required_fields_by_event:
    promotion:
      - "promoted_semantic_expression"
    deduplication:
      - "authoritative_retained_instance"
      - "deduplicated_instances"
    blocked_promotion:
      - "blocking_defect_category"
    blocked_deduplication:
      - "blocking_defect_category"

foil_defect_taxonomy:
  unauthorized_owner_selection:
    definition: "An ineligible artifact class is chosen as owner."
  semantic_collapse:
    definition: "Materially distinct semantics are flattened into one representation."
  convenience_merge:
    definition: "Merge occurs for brevity, simplicity, or output neatness rather than lawful FOIL grounds."
  destructive_deduplication:
    definition: "Deduplication deletes controlling or materially distinct meaning."
  false_promotion:
    definition: "Content is promoted without lawful shared-basis justification."
  false_deduplication:
    definition: "Content is deduplicated despite insufficient sameness or unsafe owner selection."
  ownerless_promotion:
    definition: "Promotion occurs without a single authoritative owner."
  non_owner_authority_inversion:
    definition: "Review or packaging layers are treated as authoritative owners."
  provenance_loss:
    definition: "Source lineage cannot be reconstructed after FOIL action."
  unledgered_foil_event:
    definition: "A FOIL action occurs without required ledgering."
  derivative_surface_conflation:
    definition: "Show Summary, Creative UST shell artifacts, and packaging outputs are blurred during FOIL handling."
  residual_ambiguity_requiring_downstream_reinterpretation:

    rule: "This category remains subordinate to the more specific FOIL defect categories above."

foil_block_conditions:
  blocked_if:
    - "A frozen canonical law relevant to FOIL is violated."

    - "The proposed FOIL action would collapse protected artifact distinctions."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would erase materially distinct lower-level semantics."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would functionally summarize away mechanics."

    - "The proposed FOIL action would produce provenance loss."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would be unledgered."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would require guessing missing meaning instead of preserving nulls or unresolved distinctions."

compliance_test:
```

Grade: **B+**

This is strong in structure, but I would **not** promote it yet.

## Assessment

It gets a lot right:

- infrastructure-first order is now explicitly acknowledged
- scope is mostly correct
- it avoids inventing an acronym expansion for FOIL
- it defines eligibility, anti-collapse, provenance, owner selection, and ledgering in a usable way
- it keeps the next artifact in the safer sequence: `round_robin_notes_template.yaml`, not `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`

## Why it Does Not Pass yet

The remaining problem is **canon-source overreach**.

It appears to import and harden specific FOIL behavior from "retrieved canon" that is **not clearly frozen in the approved artifact chain**. That is the main defect.

### Blocking Concerns

1. **It imports downstream ownership rules that are too specific**  
   These are the most concerning:

    - `Show Summary is the preferred downstream owner for song-wide groove, feel, emotional gravity, and narrative load…`
    - `Creative UST shell artifacts retain only the promoted content that must remain shell-addressable, parse-safe, or structurally necessary…`

    Those may be good future rules, but they read like **new canon** unless already frozen in the approved artifacts. Right now they are too specific for a FOIL governance contract.

2. **It leans on "retrieved canon" instead of the approved artifact chain**  
   The accepted source chain at this stage should be the approved artifacts:

    - `controller_contract.yaml`
    - `phase_contract.yaml`
    - `artifact_contract.yaml`
    - `controller_lockout_policy.yaml`
    - `artifact_taxonomy.md`

    If a FOIL rule is not clearly derivable from those or already frozen by the user, it should be:

    - labeled as implementation hardening, or
    - removed for now

3. **It may be deciding downstream surface distribution too early**  
   FOIL should define:

    - lawful promotion
    - lawful deduplication
    - authoritative owner selection logic
    - provenance and preservation

    It should **not yet hard-code detailed downstream expressive-load routing** unless that has already been frozen elsewhere.

---

## What Should Remain

Keep:

- FOIL as a named structural method
- no acronym guessing
- anti-collapse rules
- provenance preservation
- owner-selection logic at a general level
- no review-artifact or packaging-output ownership
- ledger obligations
- FOIL defect taxonomy
- FOIL block conditions

## What Should Be Revised

Revise or remove:

- specific `Show Summary` preference rules unless already frozen in approved artifacts
- specific `Creative UST` shell-retention rules unless already frozen
- any language that cites "retrieved canon" as authority instead of the approved artifact chain
- any downstream allocation rule that is more specific than the approved artifact set currently supports

---

## Corrective Prompt for the Other Window

Assessment revision:

This artifact is structurally strong and correctly ordered in the infrastructure-first sequence, but it is not yet promotion-ready.

Reclassify it as:

- correctly sequenced
- structurally strong
- revision required before promotion

Reason:  
The contract currently overreaches by importing FOIL behavior that is more specific than the approved artifact chain clearly freezes.

Primary blocking issue:  
canon-source overreach

Specifically revise the following:

1. Remove or reclassify downstream ownership rules that are too specific unless already frozen in approved artifacts.  
   This includes language such as:

- Show Summary as the preferred downstream owner for groove, feel, emotional gravity, and narrative load
- Creative UST shell artifacts retaining only parse-safe or structurally necessary promoted content

1. Do not rely on "retrieved canon" as an authority source here.  
   At this stage, approved authority should come from the accepted artifact chain:

- controller_contract.yaml
- phase_contract.yaml
- artifact_contract.yaml
- controller_lockout_policy.yaml
- artifact_taxonomy.md

1. Keep FOIL owner-selection logic at the lawful general level:

- choose the highest legitimate owner that preserves truth and specificity
- never select review artifacts as owners
- never select packaging outputs as owners
- do not promote if no single lawful owner can carry the semantic unit safely

1. If you want to preserve any more specific FOIL routing heuristics, move them into one of these:

- implementation_hardening_rules
- provisional_extensions  
  Do not present them as inherited canon unless already frozen explicitly.

1. Keep the infrastructure-first sequence explicit.  
   After this artifact passes, the next artifact remains:  
   round_robin_notes_template.yaml  
   Do not jump to creative_ust_canonical_shell.md yet.

Regenerate foil_promotion_contract.yaml with those corrections.  
Then re-run:

Yes. That clarification is important.

The earlier "repeat operator" proposal is **close**, but it is wrong in one key place: **application** and **detection** were collapsed into the same act.

## Corrected Reading

**Identifying repeated sections should be the first compression step.**  
Not replacing them yet. Replacing them is later.

That aligns much better with Maestro's existing logic:

- the cycle restarts by reducing character count through **concurrent co-development** of **Show Summary** and **Creative UST**  
  maestro_devsession.ai_optimized
- FOIL means duplication is **lifted, not deleted**, and deduplication happens by **abstraction under pressure, not summarization**  
  staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…
- prompt-surface compression already has deterministic priorities such as **compress non-lyric lines first**, **shorten headers before deleting content**, and **preserve Outro/End**  
  houseinorder
- Creative UST is a downstream, Suno-optimized derivative whose job is to **maximize usable content inside Suno's lyric prompt**  
  Yesterday  
  
    Yesterday

So the real issue is not "should repeat exist?"  
It is **where the system recognizes repetition, where it stores that knowledge, and when it is allowed to collapse surface text**.

## The Actual Failure Risk

The critical error in the quoted proposal is that it invites the model to think:

> "I found repeated sections, so I should immediately replace them with repeat."

That is dangerous because the system already has a known anti-pattern: when asked to save characters, it tends to:

- strip section-header detail
- genericize descriptive fields
- over-compress the non-lyric scaffolding
- believe it is "helping" by making the output shorter

That is exactly the opposite of your objective. Your objective is **use every available character for quality and nuance**, not just shorter output. The whole reason the Technical UST became so granular was to create an effectively unlimited creative surface inside a constrained container.

## Corrected Architecture

The safe model is:

### 1. Repetition Detection Comes First

Immediately after definitive `technical.ust` exists, and before final surface collapse, run a **Repeated Section Detection Pass**.

Its job is only to:

- identify repeated section blocks
- determine whether they are exact, near-exact, or structurally related
- bind them to authoritative block IDs
- record them in a compression ledger

It must **not** replace text yet.

### 2. Concurrent Surface Development Happens with Repetition-awareness

Then co-develop:

- `draft.show.summary`
- `draft.creative.ust`
- and in v5-c, persona surface as applicable

This is already the established pattern: concurrent Show Summary + Creative UST development to manage character pressure while preserving quality.

maestro_devsession.ai_optimized

staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

The difference is that now the surface compiler knows, up front, which sections are repeat-eligible later.

That gives subagents more freedom to spend characters on:

- section-header specificity
- performance notes
- production cues
- nuanced tone language
- better differentiation where uniqueness matters

### 3. FOIL Promotion Runs before Surface Collapse

Next, promote duplicate semantics from the edges inward:

- repeated song-wide traits lift upward
- duplicate global attributes move to the proper owner
- unique local detail stays local

That matches the FOIL insight: duplication is lifted upward, not deleted, and uniqueness remains preserved below.

maestro_devsession.ai_optimized

staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

### 4. Only the Final Lyric Surface May Emit Repeat Notation

Only after:

- repeated sections are detected
- surfaces are concurrently developed
- FOIL promotion is complete
- character pressure is measured

should the system decide whether to emit:

- `repeat chorus`
- `repeat pre-chorus`
- `repeat verse 1`
- `repeat chorus {2}`

And even then, only in the **final Suno lyric surface**, not in Technical UST and not in canonical Creative UST.

## Best Solution: Split "Repeat" into tWo dIfferent cOnstructs

This is the cleanest fix.

### A. Internal Construct: Repeat Candidate

Used early. Never user-facing.

Example fields:

- `block_id`
- `first_authoritative_instance`
- `matching_instances`
- `match_type: exact | near_exact | structural`
- `repeat_eligible: true|false`
- `blocked_reason`
- `char_savings_estimate`

Purpose:

- detect repeated sections first
- preserve full text
- inform later budget decisions

### B. External Construct: Repeat Operator

Used late. User/Suno-facing.

Example:

- `repeat chorus`
- `repeat chorus {2}`

Purpose:

- final-surface compression only

This split prevents the model from confusing:

- **recognition**  
   with
- **collapse**

## Corrected Order of Operations

Here is the version I would treat as canonical:

1. `technical.ust` locked
2. **Repeated Section Detection Pass**
3. concurrent development of `draft.show.summary` + `draft.creative.ust` (+ persona in v5-c)
4. FOIL promotion / deduplication from edge nodes inward
5. character-budget evaluation
6. non-lyric compression waterfall
7. final lyric-surface compression using repeat operator where allowed
8. freeze final surfaces

That ordering fits your clarification and preserves the deeper Maestro laws. It also respects the existing surface compiler behavior around dual outputs and prompt-surface constraints.

houseinorder

## Why This is Superior

### Systems / Compiler Perspective

It separates:

- analysis
- transformation
- packaging

That is much safer than one step doing all three.

### Information Theory Perspective

You do not compress until you know:

- what is globally repeated
- what is locally unique
- what is load-bearing detail

### Governance Perspective

It preserves:

- lineage
- reversibility
- addressability
- no-step-loss

### Creative Perspective

It protects exactly what you care about:

- section-header detail
- cue richness
- nuanced surface language
- maximum character usage for quality

### Human Factors / AI Behavior Perspective

It stops the model from "helpfully" shortening the wrong things.

## The Key Policy You Need

You need an explicit **compression priority law**.

Something like:

### Surface Compression Priority Law

When character pressure exists, the system shall:

1. identify repeated sections first
2. promote duplicate global semantics upward via FOIL
3. preserve section-header specificity as long as possible
4. compress non-lyric redundancy before lyric-adjacent nuance
5. shorten headers only after duplicate handling and promotion are complete
6. never genericize high-value cues merely to save tokens
7. emit repeat notation only in the final lyric surface

That directly addresses the failure mode you described.

## The Most Important Anti-failure Rule

You need one more hard rule:

### No False Efficiency Rule

**Character reduction is not success.  
Quality density per character is success.**

That is the real objective of Maestro's compression stage.

## Recommended Implementation Move

The next thing to freeze is not just a "repeat operator." It is a small governed module with three parts:

- `repeated_section_detection_contract.yaml`
- `surface_compression_priority_law.yaml`
- `repeat_operator_surface_rule.yaml`

That will keep the system from mistaking compression for simplification.

And yes: your clarification stands.  
**Repeated-section identification should be the first compression step; repeat notation should be one of the last.**

## Corrected Model

Compression in v5-c should be understood as a **worker-performed reverse pass over the finalized Technical UST**, not as something the controller "does."

The forward path is:

- user creative input
- worker population / refinement of Technical UST
- gates and deliberation
- finalized `technical.ust`

The reverse path is then:

- workers compress subkeys and variables upward into tighter key-level language
- workers co-develop the downstream triad concurrently:
  - Show Summary as director's vision
  - Creative UST as producer's vision
  - Persona as A/R artist-choice surface
- controller only orchestrates sequencing, validation, and stop/go behavior

That matches the project's existing pattern that output surfaces are derived only after the canonical object exists and after governance passes, and that dual/triple outputs are co-created rather than emitted as isolated afterthoughts.

## The Real Danger

The danger is not just "bad compression." It is **role collapse**.

If the controller is allowed to compress, it will start making semantic decisions:

- what detail is "important"
- which repeated sections are "safe" to collapse
- how much header detail to keep
- where expressive load should live

That is exactly the failure mode you called out: the AI tries to save tokens, strips section-header detail, genericizes content, and believes it is doing a good job. But the system objective is the opposite: **maximize quality density per character**, not minimize characters. The existing canon already records that over-5k handling should compress non-lyric directives first, shorten headers before deleting content, and never rewrite lyrics or lose Outro/End.

## Corrected Sequence for Compression in v5-c

Your clarification implies this order:

1. `technical.ust` finalized and locked
2. workers begin the **reverse compression pass** over Technical UST
3. workers co-develop Show Summary + Creative UST + Persona concurrently
4. duplicate terms are promoted from edge nodes inward under FOIL logic
5. repeated sections are identified early as a worker task
6. only later, if needed, surface-level shorthand such as repeat notation may be emitted in the final downstream packaging layer

This preserves the FOIL insight that duplication is **lifted, not deleted**, and that the reverse pass is structural compression with accountability, not summarization.

maestro_devsession.ai_optimized

## The Key Correction to Your Tangent

You are right:

**identifying repeated sections is the first compression step**.

But in v5-c that identification must still be performed by workers operating on the finalized Technical UST and its derived draft surfaces — not by the controller.

So the split is:

### Worker Responsibility

- detect repeated sections
- decide whether repetition is exact / near-exact / structurally repeated
- preserve performance cues and persona cues
- decide what remains local vs what promotes upward
- compress subkeys/variables into tighter key-level language
- co-balance detail across Show Summary, Creative UST, and Persona

### Controller Responsibility

- ensure this happens only after finalized `technical.ust`
- ensure workers execute in sequence
- ensure no skipped steps
- ensure lineage and ledgering
- ensure no premature packaging
- block outputs that show genericization, header stripping, or illegal collapse

## Best Solution

The clean solution is to define compression as a **worker-owned reverse compilation phase** with controller lockout.

### 1. Rename the Phase

Do not call it generic "compression."  
Call it something like:

- `worker_reverse_compilation`
- or `worker_surface_compression_pass`

That makes it clear this is not a controller optimization step.

### 2. Split the Phase into Worker Subpasses

A safe worker-only reverse pass would look like:

- **Pass A — repetition detection**
  - identify repeated section blocks first
  - no collapsing yet
- **Pass B — edge-to-root promotion**
  - promote duplicate semantics upward
  - preserve uniqueness at lower levels
- **Pass C — triad load-balancing**
  - decide what detail belongs in:
    - Show Summary
    - Creative UST
    - Persona
- **Pass D — character-budget refinement**
  - compress only after the above is settled
  - protect section-header specificity as long as possible
  - protect non-generic cues
  - never rewrite lyrics
- **Pass E — final surface shorthand**
  - only if needed, emit compact repeat notation in the final lyric surface

### 3. Add a Hard Policy

You need a policy stronger than "compress carefully":

**No False Efficiency Rule**  
Character reduction alone is not success.  
Success is **maximal retained nuance per available character**.

### 4. Add a Controller Lockout for Compression

Something like:

- controller may not perform semantic compression
- controller may not decide repeated-section equivalence
- controller may not relocate expressive load among Show Summary, Creative UST, and Persona
- controller may only validate worker outputs against:
  - phase order
  - lineage
  - parser constraints
  - budget constraints
  - anti-genericization rules

## The Most Important Architectural Insight

In v5-c, the reverse pass is not "AI shit" after Technical UST.

It is a **second worker sequence**:

- first sequence fills nulls downward into a fully specified Technical UST
- second sequence compresses upward from subkeys/variables into the triad surfaces

That is the same machine, running in reverse, with different worker duties.

## Recommended next Move

The safest next artifacts to freeze are:

- `worker_reverse_compilation_contract.yaml`
- `repeated_section_detection_contract.yaml`
- `surface_load_balancing_contract.yaml`

Those three would formalize:

- workers own compression
- repeated-section identification is step one
- character budget is used to increase nuance, not flatten output
