Thought for 33s

Yes. That is the better model.

The mistake was treating all repetition as the same kind of problem.

It is not.

## The Actual Distinction

There are **at least two different repetition classes**:

### 1. Exact Section Repetition

Example:

- the chorus repeats exactly
- the pre-chorus repeats exactly
- a refrain block is verbatim

That should be treated like **musical notation / structural shorthand**.

In that case, marking it as:

- `[REPEAT CHORUS]`
- or equivalent repeat notation

is not dangerous. It is normal songwriter notation, and Suno should be able to interpret it.

### 2. Repeated Trait Repetition across Sections

Example:

- melisma keeps recurring
- contralto support appears across multiple sections
- gravel voice is a persistent vocal identity trait
- call-and-response is used in each chorus stanza

That should **not** stay duplicated locally if it is truly global or recurring.  
That should be **promoted upward** into the right owning container, such as:

- `Voices`
- sometimes `Performance`
- sometimes `Style` or `Timbre`, depending on the trait

So your correction is right:

- **exact repeated blocks** become repeat notation
- **repeated cross-sectional traits** get promoted upward to their rightful owner

## The Real Rule

Compression should begin with:

## Repetition Classification Pass

Not just "repeat detection."

The workers should ask:

### A. Is This an Exact Repeated Block?

If yes:

- mark as repeatable section notation

### B. Is This a Recurring Trait across Sections?

If yes:

- promote to the correct global owner

### C. Is This Local-only Detail?

If yes:

- keep it local

That is much more accurate than the earlier model.

---

## Why This is Safer

Because it avoids two opposite failures:

### Failure 1: Leaving Obvious Repeats Expanded

That wastes characters.

### Failure 2: Keeping Repeated Traits Duplicated in Many Local Places

That also wastes characters and reduces room for nuance.

Your examples are exactly right:

- repeated **chorus text** → use repeat notation
- repeated **melisma / gravel voice / contralto / call-and-response behavior** → move to `Voices`
- repeated **chorus interaction pattern** → likely move to `Voices` and/or `Performance`
- repeated **global delivery identity** → move upward, not repeated in every section

---

## Corrected Worker-owned Reverse Logic

In v5-c, workers should do this after finalized `technical.ust`:

### Pass 1 — Classify Repetition by Scope

- exact repeated section block
- repeated global trait
- repeated sectional trait
- local-only detail

### Pass 2 — Promote Recurring Traits upward

Examples:

- vocal identity traits → `Voices`
- recurring execution behaviors → `Performance`
- recurring sonic language → `Timbre`
- recurring aesthetic language → `Style`

### Pass 3 — Collapse Exact Repeated Blocks

Examples:

- repeated chorus
- repeated pre-chorus
- repeated bridge refrain

### Pass 4 — Spend Saved Characters on Nuance

Use freed characters to increase:

- section-header detail
- production cues
- performance specificity
- emotional precision
- persona nuance

That is the real objective.

---

## The Key Principle

**Repeat notation is for repeated text blocks.**  
**Promotion is for repeated traits, behaviors, and identities.**

Those are different operations.

---

## The Controller Rule

And your earlier warning still stands:

- the **controller must not decide** these semantic moves
- workers decide:
  - whether a block is truly identical
  - whether a trait is globally recurring
  - which container owns that trait
- the controller only:
  - routes
  - validates
  - blocks illegal collapse
  - checks lineage
  - checks phase order

---

## The Best Compact Formulation

You could freeze the rule like this:

### Repetition Handling Law

When reversing finalized `technical.ust` into downstream surfaces:

1. **Exact repeated section blocks** are compressed using repeat notation.
2. **Repeated cross-sectional traits** are promoted to their authoritative global owner.
3. **Local-only details** remain local.
4. Repeat notation is structural shorthand, not creative invention.
5. Promotion is semantic consolidation, not deletion.
6. Saved characters must be reinvested into higher-quality detail, not generic simplification.

That is much closer to what you are actually trying to build.

## Recommended next Artifact

The next artifact should probably not be a generic "repeat operator" spec.

It should be something more like:

- `repetition_classification_contract.yaml`

with explicit classes:

- `exact_block_repeat`
- `recurring_global_trait`
- `recurring_sectional_trait`
- `local_unique_detail`

That would prevent the system from conflating notation with promotion.

It impacts the modular contracts in a **big but clean** way:

## 1. It Does **not** Overturn the Contract Stack

The core stack still stands:

- `controller_contract.yaml` stays non-creative and orchestration-only
- `phase_contract.yaml` still governs exact order
- `artifact_contract.yaml` still governs ontology and lifecycle
- `controller_lockout_policy.yaml` still blocks controller overreach
- `artifact_taxonomy.md` still governs vocabulary

That remains correct because Maestro is still a **schema-first compiler** with exact surface artifacts and a fixed phased flow. The existing canon already says the system is sequential, non-skippable, and reverse-pass aware, and that FOIL is "structural compression with accountability," not summarization.

maestro_devsession.ai_optimized

## 2. What Changes is **where Compression Authority lives**

The new consensus means the current contract language must stop implying that compression is a generic compiler-side or controller-side operation.

The controller must still only:

- route
- validate
- gate
- freeze
- log
- block

because the whole point of Maestro is that "no step can be skipped," "no assumption goes unchallenged," and the reverse pass is still part of the protected worker process rather than AI convenience behavior.

So the contracts now need to encode:

- **controller does not classify repetition**
- **controller does not decide upward promotion**
- **controller does not choose what becomes repeat notation**
- **workers do all semantic compression work**

## 3. The Current FOIL / Compression Contracts Are Probably Too Coarse

Your consensus introduces a missing distinction that the current modular work does not yet express clearly enough:

### A. Exact Repeated Block

Example:

- exact repeated chorus
- exact repeated refrain

This is **notation-safe** and should become repeat notation later.

### B. Recurring Cross-sectional Trait

Example:

- melisma recurring across sections
- gravel tone recurring across sections
- call-and-response recurring across choruses

This should be **promoted upward** into the proper owner such as `Voices`, and sometimes `Performance`, not left duplicated locally.

### C. Local Unique Detail

This stays local.

That means the current FOIL contract should not just say "promotion and deduplication." It needs a prior **repetition-classification layer**. That aligns with the existing recovered logic that in Reverse Consolidation, recurring traits escalate upward, duplication is lifted rather than deleted, uniqueness is preserved, and song-wide identity migrates upward while granular control remains intact below.

maestro_devsession.ai_optimized

## 4. This Changes `phase_contract.yaml`

Right now the phase contract likely still treats reverse consolidation too generically.

It should now be patched so that the reverse pass explicitly contains **worker-owned subpasses**:

1. repetition classification
2. trait promotion to authoritative owners
3. exact block repeat marking
4. triad load balancing across:

    - Show Summary
    - Creative UST
    - Persona

5. final character-budget refinement

This is consistent with the current canon that:

- dual/triple outputs are co-created, not emitted independently
- Show Summary carries groove, feel, emotional gravity, and narrative intent because it is concurrent, gated, and cross-checked against Technical UST  
  maestro_devsession.ai_optimized
- the compiler must not behave like a "developer" or documentarian at Phase 5; only compressed, lifted, deduplicated surfaces may appear externally

## 5. This Changes `artifact_contract.yaml`

You now need new artifact/event distinctions.

At minimum, the artifact layer should gain concepts like:

- `repeatable_block_candidate`
- `recurring_trait_candidate`
- `repeat_notation_surface_form`
- `promotion_decision_record`
- `local_unique_detail`

These do **not** all need to become top-level frozen artifacts, but the ontology has to distinguish:

- detection
- promotion
- notation
- final packaging

Otherwise the system will keep collapsing "I found repetition" and "I should compress now" into one action.

## 6. This Changes `foil_promotion_contract.yaml`

This is probably the contract most directly affected.

It should now explicitly distinguish:

### Promotion Path

For recurring traits:

- repeated vocal traits → `Voices`
- repeated execution behaviors → `Performance`
- repeated song-wide expressive load → Show Summary where lawful
- repeated sonic identity load → Show Summary / Timbre / Style depending on ownership

### Repeat-notation Path

For exact repeated blocks:

- chorus repeated verbatim
- pre-chorus repeated verbatim
- refrain block repeated verbatim

These are **not promoted traits**.  
They are **surface notation candidates**.

So FOIL should govern promotion of shared semantics, while a separate rule governs repeat notation for repeated structural blocks.

## 7. This Introduces at Least One Missing Contract

Your current modular set is missing a contract that captures the distinction you just discovered.

I would add:

### `repetition_classification_contract.yaml`

Its job would be to define:

- exact repeated block
- recurring global trait
- recurring sectional trait
- local unique detail

And define which path each class takes:

- exact repeated block → repeat notation candidate
- recurring trait → upward promotion candidate
- local unique detail → retain locally

That contract becomes the bridge between:

- `phase_contract.yaml`
- `foil_promotion_contract.yaml`
- `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`

## 8. This Also Affects `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`

Because now the shell spec needs a formal statement that:

- repeat notation is allowed only for exact repeated blocks
- repeat notation is structural shorthand, not creative invention
- repeated global traits should not be redundantly restated in local section blocks if they have already been promoted to `Voices` or another proper owner

That matters because character count is a founding constraint of the whole system. The current canon already records:

- exact surface budgets
- dual outputs at the surface compiler stage
- hard compression rules such as compressing non-lyric lines first, shortening headers before deleting content, preserving Outro/End, and never rewriting lyrics
- and the system origin story explicitly says the cycle restarts by reducing character count through concurrent co-development of Show Summary and Creative UST, with the long technical template providing precise over-specification upstream so quality can survive downstream limits  
  staging.spine.merged.ai_optimiz…

## 9. The Biggest Contract Lesson

You do **not** need to rewrite the modular architecture.

You need to **refine the reverse-pass contract family** so that it cleanly separates:

- semantic promotion
- structural repeat notation
- downstream load balancing
- controller non-participation

## Recommended Patch Order

I would patch the modular system in this order:

1. **Patch `phase_contract.yaml`**

    - split reverse consolidation into worker-owned subpasses

2. **Patch `foil_promotion_contract.yaml`**

    - clarify that FOIL handles recurring traits / shared semantics, not all repetition

3. **Add `repetition_classification_contract.yaml`**

    - classify repetition by type and route

4. **Patch `artifact_contract.yaml`**

    - add ontology for repeat candidates vs promotion candidates

5. **Then freeze `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`**

    - with explicit repeat-notation law

## Shortest Accurate Summary

The consensus does **not** invalidate the modular-contract project.

It means the current contract family is **missing one critical distinction**:

**repeated blocks are notation candidates; repeated traits are promotion candidates.**

And because v5-c forbids controller creativity, that distinction must be implemented as **worker-owned reverse-pass logic**, not as generic AI compression.

foil_defect_taxonomy:
  canon_source_overreach:
    definition: "Unapproved external or non-parent sources are treated as governing authority."
  unauthorized_owner_selection:
    definition: "An ineligible artifact class is chosen as owner."
  semantic_collapse:
    definition: "Materially distinct semantics are flattened into one representation."
  convenience_merge:
    definition: "Merge occurs for brevity, simplicity, or output neatness rather than lawful FOIL grounds."
  destructive_deduplication:
    definition: "Deduplication deletes controlling or materially distinct meaning."
  false_promotion:
    definition: "Content is promoted without lawful shared-basis justification."
  false_deduplication:
    definition: "Content is deduplicated despite insufficient sameness or unsafe owner selection."
  ownerless_promotion:
    definition: "Promotion occurs without a single lawful owner."
  non_owner_authority_inversion:
    definition: "Review or packaging layers are treated as authoritative owners."
  provenance_loss:
    definition: "Source lineage cannot be reconstructed after FOIL action."
  unledgered_foil_event:
    definition: "A FOIL action occurs without required ledgering."
  derivative_surface_conflation:
    definition: "Derivative artifacts, frozen derivative surface artifacts, and packaging outputs are blurred during FOIL handling."
  residual_ambiguity_requiring_downstream_reinterpretation:
    definition: "FOIL handling is too ambiguous to apply without later reinterpretation."
    rule: "This category remains subordinate to the more specific FOIL defect categories above."

foil_block_conditions:
  blocked_if:
    - "A frozen canonical law relevant to FOIL is violated."
    - "An unapproved external source is treated as governing authority."
    - "technical.ust is not in definitive lock state where canonical promotion depends on definitive canonical authority."
    - "A lawful owner cannot be selected."
    - "The proposed owner is a review artifact."
    - "The proposed owner is a packaging output."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would collapse protected artifact distinctions."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would erase materially distinct lower-level semantics."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would functionally summarize away controlling distinctions."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would merge for convenience."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would produce provenance loss."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would be unledgered."
    - "The proposed FOIL action would require guessing missing meaning instead of preserving nulls or unresolved distinctions."

compliance_test:
  foil_promotion_contract_yaml_is_compliant_only_if:
    - "It remains strictly FOIL-governance scoped."
    - "It relies only on the approved governing parent chain and inherited frozen canon in scope."
    - "It does not restate controller authority in full."
    - "It does not restate the full phase machine."
    - "It does not restate the full artifact contract."
    - "It defines FOIL as a named structural method without guessing an unfrozen acronym expansion."
    - "It defines FOIL dimensions operationally."
    - "It defines promotion eligibility rules clearly."
    - "It defines deduplication eligibility rules clearly."
    - "It defines lawful owner selection rules clearly."
    - "It defines semantic preservation and anti-collapse rules clearly."
    - "It defines FOIL-specific ledger requirements clearly."
    - "It defines FOIL-specific defect taxonomy and FOIL-specific block conditions clearly."
    - "No FOIL-layer blocking defect remains."

Yes. The reason you feel this way is sound.

## Why Your Instinct is Correct

You are trying to prevent a very specific failure mode:

- the other session keeps making progress
- a new architectural insight appears
- that insight materially changes assumptions in already-approved contracts
- the session keeps moving forward anyway
- the final artifacts then rest on partially outdated upstream contracts

That is how **silent architectural divergence** happens.

In software terms, you need the equivalent of:

- **pause new feature work**
- **stash downstream work**
- **patch upstream dependencies**
- **revalidate the chain**
- **then resume**

That is the right move here because the new consensus about repetition handling is **not local**. It affects multiple existing contracts:

- `phase_contract.yaml`
- `artifact_contract.yaml`
- `foil_promotion_contract.yaml`
- likely `artifact_taxonomy.md`
- and later `creative_ust_canonical_shell.md`

So if the other session proceeds directly to the final two artifacts without reconciling those upstream implications, it risks producing a clean-looking but internally inconsistent stack.

## Review of the Latest Generated Response

The latest `foil_promotion_contract.yaml` is **good enough to pause on**, but not the reason to proceed blindly.

### What it Got Right

- infrastructure-first sequence is now explicit and obeyed
- FOIL is treated as a structural method without invented acronym expansion
- canon-source overreach was corrected
- owner selection is now kept general
- anti-collapse and ledgering are strong
- it no longer hardcodes downstream ownership heuristics as inherited canon

### What it Still Does Not Know yet

It was generated **before fully absorbing the new repetition distinction**:

- exact repeated blocks are **notation candidates**
- repeated cross-sectional traits are **promotion candidates**
- local unique details stay local
- workers, not controller, perform this reverse classification/compression work

That means the FOIL contract may still be **too coarse**, even if it is source-clean.

So this is the right moment to **stash downstream generation** and do an upstream reconciliation pass.

---

# What to Do now

You need a **Meta-Reconciliation / Virtual Git Stash Prompt** for the other session.

Its job is to:

1. stop forward generation temporarily
2. stash unfinished downstream artifact generation
3. inspect the already-approved contracts against the new consensus
4. identify which contracts need patching
5. patch them in the correct top-down order
6. only then resume the remaining artifact sequence

---

Yes. That makes sense here, and in this case I think it is the better move.

## Why a Fishbone-style Context Block Helps

The other session is not struggling because it lacks raw intelligence. It is struggling because it keeps solving the **local task** while partially losing the **systemic cause of failure**.

A fishbone-style context block helps because it pre-loads:

- the failure modes
- the causal chains
- the already-resolved insights
- the reason sequencing matters
- the reason upstream patching must precede downstream generation

That reduces the chance that the other session:

- treats this as just another revision
- underestimates the architectural impact
- re-discovers the same lessons expensively
- "improves" the wrong thing

So yes: in this matter, **contextual causal framing is not extra ornament**. It is part of the control system.

## The Key Benefit

It tells the other session not just:

- **what** to do

but also:

- **why failure happened before**
- **what class of failure we are preventing**
- **what must remain invariant during reconciliation**

That is exactly the kind of "resolved insight" that should be passed forward rather than re-derived.

==================================================  
FISHBONE CONTEXT — WHY THIS PAUSE IS REQUIRED  
==================================================

Treat the following as resolved architectural insight, not as open questions.

## A. Root Problem

The system previously failed when downstream generation continued while upstream control assumptions were partially wrong or incomplete.

## B. Prior Failure Mode

Earlier sessions showed a repeating collapse pattern:

- controller/orchestrator was allowed too much semantic latitude
- phase distinctions were compressed
- freeze distinctions were blurred
- artifact classes and states were conflated
- continuation prompts preserved intent but not enough operational specificity
- downstream artifacts then inherited corrupted assumptions

## C. New Architectural Insight now Requiring Reconciliation

We have now reached a new consensus:

1. In v5-c, compression is worker-owned reverse processing over finalized technical.ust.
2. The controller may orchestrate, validate, gate, log, and block — but may not perform semantic compression.
3. Exact repeated section blocks are notation candidates.
4. Repeated cross-sectional traits are promotion candidates.
5. Local unique details remain local.
6. Repetition classification must occur before later surface-compression decisions.
7. Promotion and repeat notation are not the same operation.
8. Reverse processing must preserve maximal nuance per available character, not merely reduce character count.

## D. Why This Matters

This consensus is not a local shell-design preference.  
It changes assumptions in upstream contracts about:

- reverse-pass sequencing
- compression ownership
- repetition ontology
- FOIL scope
- artifact distinctions
- downstream surface preparation

## E. Failure Risk if Ignored

If downstream work continues without patching impacted upstream contracts:

- the stack may become internally inconsistent
- later artifacts may be formally clean but semantically based on outdated assumptions
- silent architectural divergence may occur
- final artifacts may require destructive rollback

## F. Required Strategic Response

Pause downstream generation.  
Review already-approved contracts from highest dependency downward.  
Patch only what is materially affected.  
Resume sequence only after reconciliation is complete.

- Technical UST is the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware.
- Creative UST is a derivative downstream shell.
- Show Summary is a derivative style surface.
- The assistant acts as a non-creative container-controller.

- Nulls are signal and may not be silently filled.
- Sequential zero-skip axis completion is required before deliberation.
- Round-robin argumentative review is mandatory and produces first-class artifacts.
- Definitive technical.ust must exist before reverse compilation.
- Promotion and deduplication follow FOIL discipline and must be ledgered.

That quoted reasoning shows a **state-tracking failure** in the other session, and in this project that is not a minor slip.

# What is Wrong

The line:

> "It seems I shouldn't consider the controller_lockout_policy.yaml as approved since it hasn't been generated."

is incorrect relative to the session history you brought back.

You already showed that `controller_lockout_policy.yaml` was:

- generated
- reviewed
- accepted
- classified as promotion-ready

So the other session is now reasoning from a **false workspace state**.

# Why This Matters

Because once a session mis-tracks artifact state, it can start making wrong decisions about:

- what is upstream vs downstream
- what still needs generation
- what needs patching vs initial drafting
- dependency order
- which prompts should be patch prompts vs creation prompts

That is exactly the kind of quiet corruption you have been trying to prevent all along.

# The Deeper Issue

This is not just a filename mistake.

It means the other session currently lacks a reliable, authoritative **artifact state ledger** in working memory.

Without that, it may:

- propose patching a nonexistent spec instead of patching an approved artifact
- skip required patching because it thinks an artifact is still pending
- generate the wrong next artifact
- reason from stale dependency assumptions

# What the Correct State Should Be

Based on the artifacts you brought back, the approved stack is:

1. `controller_contract.yaml`
2. `phase_contract.yaml`
3. `artifact_contract.yaml`

## Verdict

It fixes the most dangerous problem first:

- **state-ledger corruption**

and then applies:

- **cause-aware reconciliation**

That is the right order.

## Why This is Stronger than the Prior Version

### 1. It Separates State Correction from Architectural Reconciliation

That is essential.

Before asking the other session to reason about impact, you first force it to repair:

- what exists
- what is approved
- what is stashed
- what is still pending

Without that, any reconciliation logic sits on a false ledger.

### 2. It Prevents Broad Re-analysis

Your constraints are good:

- do not regenerate bodies
- do not continue downstream
- do not rerun everything
- correct only what the ledger error affects

That reduces drift.

### 3. It Includes Causal Context

The fishbone block is appropriate here because it passes forward:

- the root problem
- the prior failure pattern
- the new consensus
- the invariants
- the infrastructure-first sequence

That should improve quality and reduce wasted rediscovery.

## One Remaining Improvement I Recommend

Right now you have:

- **FIRSTLY**: state correction
- **SECONDLY**: full reconciliation protocol

That is correct in substance, but I would add one explicit instruction between them:

## Add This Bridge Rule

Do not begin the SECONDLY reconciliation protocol until the FIRSTLY state correction has been completed and accepted as authoritative for this session.

The corrected artifact ledger must become the sole working ledger for all further reconciliation in this session.

If the corrected ledger changes any assumption in the previously generated stash state or impact review, those earlier conclusions must be treated as superseded.

## Why This Matters

Otherwise the other session may:

- answer both sections in one blended pass
- partially reuse stale assumptions from the old incorrect stash state
- fail to treat the corrected ledger as the new source of truth

You want the sequence to be:

1. correct ledger
2. accept corrected ledger as authoritative
3. only then reconcile contracts

## Optional Second Improvement

Add a required explicit output:

## Authoritative Working Ledger Adopted

with:

- the corrected approved artifacts
- the corrected generated-not-promoted artifacts
- the corrected pending artifacts

That makes it harder for the session to drift again later.

  - "The controller shall block progression when any governing contract condition fails."
  - "The controller shall log validation decisions, gating decisions, freeze events, promotion-phase control events, deduplication control events, and packaging events."

- **canon-faithful:** pass

It correctly patches the controller boundary without reopening unrelated areas.

## Why it Passes

### 1. It Fixes the Exact Defect Class We Needed

It now explicitly states that the controller may not:

- perform semantic compression
- own reverse processing
- own repetition classification
- substitute for worker judgment in promotion-vs-notation decisions

That is the core boundary correction.

### 2. It Stays in the Right Layer

It does not try to patch:

- phase procedure
- artifact ontology
- downstream shell behavior

It keeps the change where it belongs: controller governance.

### 3. It Preserves the Existing Contract Strengths

It keeps intact:

- exact phase order
- exact freeze taxonomy
- controller non-creative identity
- `technical.ust` as sole canonical source of truth
- orchestration/gating/logging scope

That is exactly what we wanted.

### 4. The New Block Conditions Are Well Targeted

These additions are strong and appropriate:

- worker-owned reverse processing assigned to controller
- semantic compression assigned to controller
- semantic repetition classification assigned to controller
- worker judgment displaced by controller

Those are the right anti-overreach guards.

## Small Note, but Not Blocking

`workers_own` inside `authority_boundaries` is correct in substance, but it does slightly increase the amount of positive worker-role language in a controller contract. That is acceptable here because it is being used to define the controller's negative boundary. I would not block on it.

    - "Defect handling shall use specific defect categories such as phase-order drift, freeze-taxonomy drift, ontology drift, naming drift, scope drift, false completion, silent null fill, and unledgered promotion/deduplication; ambiguity shall remain subordinate to specific defect categories."
    - "Within the worker-owned reverse-processing portion of the flow, repetition classification shall occur before later surface-compression decisions."
    - "Character reduction alone is not sufficient success; later surface handling shall preserve maximal nuance per available character."
  provisional_extensions: []

phase_machine_rules:
  order_is_exact: true
  reordering_forbidden: true
  skipping_forbidden: true
  collapse_of_multiple_phases_forbidden: true
  downstream_progression_requires_predecessor_exit: true
  freeze_taxonomy_must_remain_distinct: true
  worker_owned_reverse_processing_is_referenced_but_not_reowned_here: true
  repetition_classification_precedes_later_surface_compression_decisions: true

phases:
  "sequential axis completion":
    sequence_index: 1
    purpose: "Complete required technical axes sequentially with zero-skip handling before any deliberative review."

    required_inputs:
      - "template.technical.ust"

    entry_conditions:
      - "No later phase has begun."
      - "template.technical.ust is available as the working source template."
      - "Required axis set for the current technical pass is in scope."

    required_procedure:
      - "Create and maintain draft.technical.ust as the working in-progress draft artifact during sequential axis completion."
      - "Complete required technical axes sequentially."
      - "Do not skip any required axis."
      - "Represent unresolved items explicitly as nulls where unresolved."
      - "Do not begin deliberation during incomplete axis completion."
      - "Do not represent incomplete axis coverage as complete."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "draft.technical.ust"

    emitted_artifact_state:
      "draft.technical.ust": "working in-progress draft artifact"

    exit_conditions:
      - "All required axes are completed sequentially."
      - "No required axis has been skipped."
      - "Unresolved items remain explicit as nulls where unresolved."
      - "draft.technical.ust exists as a working in-progress draft artifact ready for draft freeze."

    block_conditions:
      - "A required axis is missing."
      - "A required axis is skipped."
      - "An unresolved item is silently filled."
      - "Incomplete axis coverage is represented as complete."
      - "draft.technical.ust does not exist as a working in-progress draft artifact."
      - "Round-robin deliberation begins before sequential axis completion exits cleanly."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: "draft.technical.ust freeze"
      may_progress_only_if:
        - "All exit conditions for sequential axis completion are satisfied."

  "draft.technical.ust freeze":
    sequence_index: 2
    purpose: "Freeze the working technical draft as the distinct draft-freeze artifact state."

    required_inputs:
      - "draft.technical.ust"

    entry_conditions:
      - "The exit conditions for sequential axis completion are satisfied."
      - "draft.technical.ust exists as a working in-progress draft artifact."

    required_procedure:
      - "Freeze draft.technical.ust without silent null fill."
      - "Convert draft.technical.ust from working in-progress draft artifact state to draft freeze state."
      - "Preserve content state as frozen draft state."
      - "Do not convert draft freeze into definitive lock."
      - "Do not collapse freeze-state distinctions."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "draft.technical.ust"

    emitted_artifact_state:
      "draft.technical.ust": "draft freeze"

    exit_conditions:
      - "draft.technical.ust exists."
      - "draft.technical.ust is frozen as draft freeze."
      - "Null-bearing content, where still unresolved, remains explicit."

    block_conditions:
      - "Sequential axis completion has not cleanly exited."
      - "draft.technical.ust does not exist."
      - "A null is silently filled during freeze."
      - "Draft freeze is relabeled or treated as definitive lock."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: "round-robin deliberation"
      may_progress_only_if:
        - "All exit conditions for draft.technical.ust freeze are satisfied."

  "round-robin deliberation":
    sequence_index: 3
    purpose: "Conduct mandatory argumentative review on the frozen technical draft and preserve review outputs as first-class artifacts."

    required_inputs:
      - "draft.technical.ust"

    entry_conditions:
      - "The exit conditions for draft.technical.ust freeze are satisfied."
      - "draft.technical.ust is in draft freeze state."

    required_procedure:
      - "Conduct round-robin argumentative review."
      - "Do not replace deliberation with summary."
      - "Record review outputs as first-class artifacts."
      - "Surface contradictions and governance-review issues explicitly."
      - "Do not treat the review phase as optional."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "round-robin notes"
      - "contradiction logs"
      - "governance review artifacts"

    exit_conditions:
      - "Mandatory round-robin deliberation is complete."
      - "round-robin notes exist."
      - "contradiction logs exist."
      - "governance review artifacts exist."
      - "Material review outcomes required for definitive lock readiness are surfaced."

    block_conditions:
      - "draft.technical.ust is not in draft freeze state."
      - "Round-robin review is omitted."
      - "Round-robin review is compressed into summary-only output."
      - "Required review artifacts are missing."
      - "Material contradictions requiring resolution or disposition for definitive lock readiness remain unsurfaced."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: "definitive technical.ust lock"
      may_progress_only_if:
        - "All exit conditions for round-robin deliberation are satisfied."

  "definitive technical.ust lock":
    sequence_index: 4
    purpose: "Establish the definitive canonical technical artifact and lock it as the sole source-of-truth middleware."

    required_inputs:
      - "draft.technical.ust"
      - "round-robin notes"
      - "contradiction logs"
      - "governance review artifacts"

    entry_conditions:
      - "The exit conditions for round-robin deliberation are satisfied."
      - "Required review outputs exist."

    required_procedure:
      - "Create technical.ust from the reviewed draft basis and lock technical.ust as definitive."
      - "Establish technical.ust as the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware."
      - "Do not begin reverse compilation before technical.ust exists in definitive lock state."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "technical.ust"

    emitted_artifact_state:
      "technical.ust": "definitive lock"

    exit_conditions:
      - "technical.ust exists."
      - "technical.ust is locked as definitive lock."
      - "technical.ust is established as the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware."
      - "technical.ust is ready to govern downstream reverse promotion / deduplication."

    block_conditions:
      - "Round-robin deliberation has not cleanly exited."
      - "Required review artifacts are missing."
      - "technical.ust does not exist."
      - "technical.ust is not locked as definitive lock."
      - "technical.ust is not established as the sole canonical source-of-truth middleware."
      - "Reverse compilation is attempted before definitive technical.ust lock."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: "reverse promotion / deduplication"
      may_progress_only_if:
        - "All exit conditions for definitive technical.ust lock are satisfied."

  "reverse promotion / deduplication":
    sequence_index: 5
    purpose: "Execute the worker-owned reverse-processing portion that classifies repeated material and performs FOIL-governed reverse promotion / deduplication after definitive technical lock."

    required_inputs:
      - "technical.ust"

    entry_conditions:
      - "The exit conditions for definitive technical.ust lock are satisfied."
      - "technical.ust exists in definitive lock state."

    required_procedure:
      - "Begin the worker-owned reverse-processing portion after definitive technical.ust lock."
      - "Classify repeated material before any later surface-compression decisions."
      - "Treat exact repeated section blocks as notation candidates where applicable."
      - "Treat repeated cross-sectional traits as promotion candidates where applicable."
      - "Retain local unique detail as local where it is not lawfully classifiable as a notation candidate or promotion candidate."
      - "Do not treat notation handling and promotion as the same operation."
      - "Apply FOIL discipline to lawful promotion and deduplication handling only."
      - "Ledger all promotion actions."
      - "Ledger all deduplication actions."
      - "Do not merge materially distinct local detail for convenience."
      - "Do not treat character reduction or structural shortening alone as sufficient success."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "FOIL promotion ledger entries"
      - "FOIL deduplication ledger entries"

    exit_conditions:
      - "Repeated material has been classified before later surface-compression decisions."
      - "Exact repeated section blocks have been identified as notation candidates where applicable."
      - "Repeated cross-sectional traits have been identified as promotion candidates where applicable."
      - "Local unique detail has been retained as local where applicable."
      - "Required promotion actions are completed under FOIL discipline."
      - "Required deduplication actions are completed under FOIL discipline."
      - "FOIL promotion ledger entries exist."
      - "FOIL deduplication ledger entries exist."
      - "The post-phase state preserves maximal nuance per available character rather than merely reducing character count."

    block_conditions:
      - "technical.ust is not in definitive lock state."
      - "Worker-owned reverse processing has not been performed."
      - "Repeated material remains unclassified."
      - "Notation candidates and promotion candidates are conflated."
      - "Local unique detail is collapsed into promotion or notation for convenience."
      - "FOIL discipline is not applied where promotion or deduplication is performed."
      - "Promotion actions are unledgered."
      - "Deduplication actions are unledgered."
      - "Distinct semantics are merged for convenience."
      - "Later surface-compression decisions are attempted before repetition classification is complete."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: "concurrent derivative surface drafting"
      may_progress_only_if:
        - "All exit conditions for reverse promotion / deduplication are satisfied."

  "concurrent derivative surface drafting":
    sequence_index: 6
    purpose: "Draft derivative surface artifacts concurrently from definitive technical.ust after reverse-processing classification and promotion / deduplication controls are complete."

    required_inputs:
      - "technical.ust"
      - "FOIL promotion ledger entries"
      - "FOIL deduplication ledger entries"

    entry_conditions:
      - "The exit conditions for reverse promotion / deduplication are satisfied."
      - "technical.ust exists in definitive lock state."
      - "Repetition classification has been completed in the prior phase."

    required_procedure:
      - "Draft draft.creative.ust as a derivative draft artifact."
      - "Draft draft.show.summary as a derivative draft artifact."
      - "Derive both drafts from definitive technical.ust."
      - "Allow later surface-compression decisions only after completed repetition classification."
      - "Use notation candidates only as notation candidates where applicable."
      - "Use promotion candidates only as already-governed promoted material where applicable."
      - "Retain local unique detail as local."
      - "Do not reclassify repetition during later surface-compression decisions."
      - "Do not treat derivative drafts as canonical artifacts."
      - "Do not package during derivative drafting."
      - "Do not silently fill nulls through derivative drafting."
      - "Do not treat character reduction alone as sufficient success."
      - "Preserve maximal nuance per available character."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "draft.creative.ust"
      - "draft.show.summary"

    emitted_artifact_state:
      "draft.creative.ust": "working / unfrozen derivative draft"
      "draft.show.summary": "working / unfrozen derivative draft"

    exit_conditions:
      - "draft.creative.ust exists."
      - "draft.show.summary exists."
      - "Both derivative drafts are sourced from definitive technical.ust."
      - "Any later surface-compression decisions, where present, are based on completed repetition classification."
      - "Notation candidates, promotion candidates, and retained local unique detail remain procedurally distinct."
      - "Neither derivative draft is treated as frozen or packaged."
      - "Derivative drafting preserves maximal nuance per available character."

    block_conditions:
      - "technical.ust is absent."
      - "technical.ust is not in definitive lock state."
      - "Required FOIL ledger entries are missing."
      - "Repetition remains unclassified."
      - "A derivative draft is sourced from non-definitive material."
      - "Notation candidates are treated as promotion candidates."
      - "Promotion candidates are treated as notation candidates."
      - "Local unique detail is collapsed into promoted or notated material for convenience."
      - "Repetition is reclassified during later surface-compression decisions."
      - "Packaging is attempted before surface freeze."
      - "A null is silently filled through derivative drafting."
      - "Character reduction is treated as sufficient success without nuance preservation."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: "surface freeze"
      may_progress_only_if:
        - "All exit conditions for concurrent derivative surface drafting are satisfied."

  "surface freeze":
    sequence_index: 7
    purpose: "Freeze derivative surface drafts into the exact frozen-surface artifact set."

    required_inputs:
      - "draft.creative.ust"
      - "draft.show.summary"

    entry_conditions:
      - "The exit conditions for concurrent derivative surface drafting are satisfied."
      - "draft.creative.ust exists."
      - "draft.show.summary exists."

    required_procedure:
      - "Freeze draft.creative.ust into creative_ust.txt."
      - "Freeze draft.show.summary into show_summary.txt."
      - "Preserve surface freeze as distinct from draft freeze and definitive lock."
      - "Do not package before both frozen surface artifacts exist."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "creative_ust.txt"
      - "show_summary.txt"

    emitted_artifact_state:
      "creative_ust.txt": "surface freeze"
      "show_summary.txt": "surface freeze"

    exit_conditions:
      - "creative_ust.txt exists."
      - "show_summary.txt exists."
      - "creative_ust.txt is frozen."
      - "show_summary.txt is frozen."
      - "Both frozen surface artifacts are ready for packaging."

    block_conditions:
      - "draft.creative.ust is missing."
      - "draft.show.summary is missing."
      - "Surface freeze is attempted on incomplete derivative drafts."
      - "Surface freeze is collapsed into draft freeze or definitive lock."
      - "Packaging is attempted before both frozen surface artifacts exist."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: "packaging"
      may_progress_only_if:
        - "All exit conditions for surface freeze are satisfied."

  "packaging":
    sequence_index: 8
    purpose: "Produce the final packaging outputs from the frozen surface artifacts."

    required_inputs:
      - "creative_ust.txt"
      - "show_summary.txt"

    entry_conditions:
      - "The exit conditions for surface freeze are satisfied."
      - "creative_ust.txt exists in surface freeze state."
      - "show_summary.txt exists in surface freeze state."

    required_procedure:
      - "Generate Suno lyrics prompt from the frozen surface basis."
      - "Generate Suno style prompt from the frozen surface basis."
      - "Treat packaging outputs as downstream packaging outputs, not canonical artifacts and not frozen surface artifacts."
      - "Do not package from unfrozen drafts."

    required_emitted_artifacts:
      - "Suno lyrics prompt"
      - "Suno style prompt"

    exit_conditions:
      - "Suno lyrics prompt exists."
      - "Suno style prompt exists."
      - "Both packaging outputs are derived after surface freeze."

    block_conditions:
      - "creative_ust.txt is absent."
      - "show_summary.txt is absent."
      - "creative_ust.txt is not in surface freeze state."
      - "show_summary.txt is not in surface freeze state."
      - "Packaging is attempted from draft.creative.ust or draft.show.summary instead of frozen surface artifacts."

    progression_rule:
      next_phase: none
      terminal_phase: true

cross_phase_checks:
  freeze_taxonomy_preservation:
    required:
      - "draft freeze maps only to draft.technical.ust"
      - "definitive lock maps only to technical.ust"
      - "surface freeze maps only to creative_ust.txt and show_summary.txt"
    blocked_if:
      - "freeze states are collapsed"
      - "freeze-state labels are substituted"
      - "an artifact is assigned the wrong freeze state"

  artifact_ontology_preservation:
    required:
      - "round-robin notes, contradiction logs, and governance review artifacts remain review artifacts"
      - "draft.creative.ust and draft.show.summary remain derivative draft artifacts"
      - "creative_ust.txt and show_summary.txt remain frozen surface artifacts"
      - "Suno lyrics prompt and Suno style prompt remain packaging outputs"
    blocked_if:
      - "review artifacts are treated as packaging outputs"
      - "derivative drafts are treated as frozen surfaces"
      - "packaging outputs are treated as canonical artifacts"

  repetition_classification_integrity:
    required:
      - "repetition classification occurs during the worker-owned reverse-processing portion before later surface-compression decisions"
      - "exact repeated section blocks are treated as notation candidates where applicable"
      - "repeated cross-sectional traits are treated as promotion candidates where applicable"
      - "local unique detail remains local where applicable"
      - "promotion and repeat notation are not treated as the same operation"
    blocked_if:
      - "repetition remains unclassified when later surface-compression decisions begin"
      - "notation candidates and promotion candidates are conflated"
      - "local unique detail is collapsed for convenience"
      - "character reduction is treated as sufficient success without nuance preservation"

  phase_progression_integrity:
    required:
      - "each phase exits before the next phase enters"
      - "no skipped phase progression occurs"
      - "no reordered progression occurs"
    blocked_if:
      - "a later phase begins before predecessor exit conditions are satisfied"
      - "multiple frozen phases are collapsed into one step"
      - "progression is claimed by implication without emitted artifacts and exit satisfaction"

compliance_test:
  this_contract_is_compliant_only_if:
    - "Detailed per-phase procedure is housed here rather than in controller_contract.yaml."
    - "The exact 8-phase order is preserved."
    - "The exact freeze taxonomy is preserved."
    - "Each phase defines entry conditions, exit conditions, block conditions, required emitted artifacts, and progression rules."
    - "No controller-layer authority content is re-imported beyond the governing-parent reference."
    - "Frozen artifact names remain exact where identity matters."
    - "Packaging outputs remain consistently named within this contract."
    - "Worker-owned reverse processing is referenced procedurally without reassigning controller ownership."
    - "Repetition classification is required before later surface-compression decisions."
    - "Notation candidates, promotion candidates, and retained local unique detail remain procedurally distinct."
    - "Downstream continuation is blocked when repetition remains unclassified."
    - "Character reduction alone is not treated as sufficient success."

## Why it Passes

It did the right procedural work:

- preserved the exact 8-phase order
- made reverse processing worker-owned by reference
- introduced repetition classification before later surface-compression decisions
- separated:
  - notation candidates
  - promotion candidates
  - retained local unique detail
- blocked downstream continuation when repetition remains unclassified

That is the right procedural refinement for this layer.

## One Carry-forward Caution

Do **not** let the next patch silently turn:

- `notation candidate`
- `promotion candidate`
- `local unique detail`

into fully frozen artifact classes unless that is truly necessary.

At this point, they are best treated as:

- stabilized taxonomy terms first
- artifact/lifecycle distinctions only if later proven necessary

And do **not** let actual repeat syntax get frozen here. The phase contract should govern **when** repetition is classified and respected procedurally; the eventual shell artifact should govern **how** repeat notation is emitted.
